Owner decision, 2026-10-09: the engine **links** those files. Moving them into the engine would move `0051`'s shipping copy out from under `init`; copying them would be a second copy to drift; having the tool inject them into the engine would put a seam with one caller in front of the check. The manifest under `src/tool/manifest/v0.1.0/` is not linked: the check reads only the on-disk manifest (C-4).

**The folder gives the version (item `0126`, decision 0009; B-086, B-087, C-6, C-16).** Every shipping tree uses `v<major.minor.patch>`, including the existing `manifest/` home, whose placement remains OQ-52. `SchemaVersions.ReadEmbedded` parses the segment after `schema/v` with `SemanticVersion.TryParse`; no folder-to-version mapping remains. The `$id` of each embedded and live frontmatter schema carries the same segment before `/frontmatter/<file>`. Rule pages carry `0.1.0` in their Schema version row and embed under `rules/v0.1.0/`. `InitWriter` uses the same value type to select the newest shipping folder by major, minor and patch (`0001-F4` § 7); it copies the bytes unchanged. This does not change `init`'s manifest pin (item `0049`) or redesign the vocabulary (§ 5 row 25).

**Init pins the newest shipped version (B-012).** Reuse: [`InitWriter.Newest`](../Features/Init/InitWriter.cs) already parses the shipping folder names with `SemanticVersion`, takes their maximum, and copies only that version's files. The selected shipping manifest supplies its `schemaVersion` string; init does not synthesize or rewrite the pin. The unit tier reads the written pin across major, minor and patch boundaries and unordered inputs; the acceptance scenario exercises `0.9.0` versus `0.10.0`, and a process test verifies that the real embedded manifest pins the newest engine version. The newest shipping manifest, `0.2.0`, and its live copy explicitly carry `schemaVersion: "0.2.0"`; locked `0.1.0` and its digest remain unchanged. The working `0.1.1` that [decision 0010](decisions/0010-init-pins-a-new-working-schema-version.md) added for this stopped shipping under [decision 0013](decisions/0013-schema-0-1-1-stops-shipping.md). The writer stays unchanged.

**The rule vocabulary is written per version.** Version `0.1.0`'s twenty-one ids (brief § 4) are a hand-written list inside `SchemaVersions.Shipped`, in the arm matching that `SemanticVersion`; an embedded `schema/v<major.minor.patch>/` with no arm fails on first read rather than checking with an empty vocabulary. The rules that run are the hand-written list of `ISpecRule` registrations in `AddSpechtEngine()` (`0001-F1` § 7, item `0107`), which replaced the static list of item `0106`, itself the replacement for reflection over the assembly. Neither item moved the vocabulary: it stands beside the rules, in `SchemaVersions.Shipped`, not inside them, no rule says which version introduced it, and no item moves it. `0042` does not do `0105`-`0107`'s work.

**The pin (B-002, B-039).** [`SpecManifest`](https://github.com/RLittlesII/specht/blob/97277d3c85f59a4b0a3b3973ee31b492f9adb1ee/src/specht/Manifest/SpecManifest.cs) knows the key `schemaVersion`. No key reads as `0.1.0`, the version that predates it (A-1). A JSON string `SemanticVersion.TryParse` accepts loads, even one the tool does not ship: rejecting an unshipped pin is the version set's job, not the loader's. Anything else - the JSON number `1` the retired pin was, the strings `"1"`, `"0.1"` and `"v0.1.0"`, a boolean, `null`, an array - is a `SpechtManifestException` naming the manifest's repository-relative path and the value as the manifest's JSON writes it, a string in its quotes, so `1` and `"1"` read differently in the message. The pin travels on [`SpecStructure`](../../specht/Manifest/SpecStructure.cs)`.SchemaVersion`.

**Loading with the pinned version (B-001, C-4).**
[`SpecSchemasLoader`](../../specht/Model/SpecSchemasLoader.cs) reads the consumer
manifest, validates its pin, and compiles that version's embedded schemas before
resolving the source as described above. Its disk path uses the manifest store,
retains the pin and vocabulary, and marks the compiled result `Disk`. The
root-only entry points preserve automatic embedded/upstream selection. The
removed direct-file `SpecSchemas.Load` overload has no remaining caller.

**The vocabulary decides the run (B-014).** Item `0042` built the filter in [`SpechtRunner.Run(string, SchemaVersions)`](../../specht/SpechtRunner.cs), which selects the pinned version a second time from the loaded structure. From item `0105` ([ADR-0008](../../../.spec/adr/0008-evaluate-takes-the-rule-set-and-the-model-carries-the-vocabulary.md); built 2026-10-09) the filter's home is `SpechtRunner.Evaluate(SpecModel, IEnumerable<ISpecRule>)`, and `Run` holds none. `Evaluate` reads the vocabulary from `model.Schemas.Version.RuleIds`, evaluates each rule it is given that reports at least one id in it, drops any violation whose rule id is outside it, and counts as evaluated only the vocabulary's ids among the evaluated rules' `ReportedIds`. The rule is the same and so is every verdict; it now holds for any caller of `Evaluate`, not only for `Run`. `Run(string, SchemaVersions)` loads the model and passes the discovered rules; `Run(string)` is `Run(string, SchemaVersions.Embedded)`, so the host's `Func<string, SpechtReport>` registration in `Program.cs` is unchanged. The signatures are in `0001-F1` § 7.

**The report names the version.** [`SpechtReport`](../../specht/SpechtReport.cs) carries `SchemaVersion`, a `SemanticVersion`, as its first positional member, and [`SpecReportDocument.From`](../../specht/Report/SpecReportDocument.cs) writes its `major.minor.patch` text with the source carried by the report (`0001-F3` B-005, § 7 there). Item `0092` adds `SchemaSource`: `embedded` without a recorded source and `upstream` with one. Explicit source selection remains item `0043`'s.

**Version `0.1.0`'s epic schema (B-022, B-023).** Already in the shipping copy (decision 0003, item `0051`): optional `title` and `description` with `minLength: 1`. `0042` only makes the check read it from the embedded set, so an empty value is a `SPEC004` through the existing frontmatter rule.

**The upstream schema source is a record on the structure (item `0176`, decision 0008; built 2026-10-11).** [`SpecManifest`](https://github.com/RLittlesII/specht/blob/97277d3c85f59a4b0a3b3973ee31b492f9adb1ee/src/specht/Manifest/SpecManifest.cs) adds `spechtSchema` to the keys it knows and reads it beside the pin, onto [`SpecStructure`](../../specht/Manifest/SpecStructure.cs)`.SpechtSchema`, which is null when the manifest has no such key. Two records join `src/specht/Manifest/`. [`UpstreamSchemaSource`](../../specht/Manifest/UpstreamSchemaSource.cs) holds `Location` and `Version` as the manifest wrote them, and `Files`. [`UpstreamSchemaFile`](../../specht/Manifest/UpstreamSchemaFile.cs) is one fetched kind: its `Kind`, the `frontmatterSchemas` key; its `FileName`, that key's value after the default fill (`0001-F5` B-008, B-019); its `Address`; and its `Hash`. `Files` holds exactly the fetched kinds, in the order `feature`, `task`, `epic`, so "which kinds are fetched" and "each kind's address" are values on the model and nothing computes them a second time. Both records are data: neither loads or parses itself, and the property is named for its key as the structure's other members are. Items `0092`, `0093` and `0095` read them from `SpecStructure`; this item adds no reader, no store, no registration and no network type (OQ-39 is theirs).

**Where the read sits.** In `SpecManifest`'s existing parse, as private members beside `SchemaVersion` and `IdentityFaults`, the way items `0014` and `0120` added their keys. That adds to a static class [ADR-0010](../../../.spec/adr/0010-layered-engine-boundaries-with-io-at-the-edge-and-a-mediator.md) removes in its stage 5; it adds no static class, no non-private static member and no file access, and it moves to `SpecManifestParser` with the rest of the parse. A parser of its own for one key would be a second manifest parse beside the one stage 5 moves.

**Every rejection is code (B-069 to B-073).** The manifest file is data, not a JSON Schema, and nothing validates it against one, so there is no schema for a rejection to sit in. The faults join the list `Check` already collects, so a manifest with several is rejected once, each named, as one `SpechtManifestException` carrying the manifest's repository-relative path; `CheckCommand`'s catch is unchanged (`0001-F5` B-022, B-023).

| Given                                                                                           | Fault, after the manifest's path                                                                                      | Claim        |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ |
| no `location`, `version` or `hashes` member                                                     | `spechtSchema has no 'location'`, one fault for each absent member                                                    | B-069        |
| a `location` that is not a string, or not an absolute address whose scheme is `https`           | `the 'location' of spechtSchema is not an https address`                                                              | B-070        |
| a `version` that is not a string, or is the empty string                                        | `the 'version' of spechtSchema is not a non-empty string`                                                             | B-071        |
| `hashes` with no entry for a fetched kind                                                       | `the 'hashes' of spechtSchema holds no entry for the kind 'task'`                                                     | B-072, B-076 |
| a fetched kind's entry that is not a string of `sha256:` and 64 digits, each `0`-`9` or `a`-`f` | `the 'hashes' entry of spechtSchema for the kind 'task' is not 'sha256:' followed by 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits` | B-073        |

No fault repeats the value. A `location` that is a folder on the machine is an absolute path, and no output carries one (brief § 9); the member or the kind is what the claims ask to be named. An absent member raises only its own fault, not the ones that would read it. A `spechtSchema` that is not a JSON object, a `hashes` that is not one, and a JSON `null` in the key's place take the path every other key of the wrong JSON type takes: `SpechtManifestUnreadableException`, exit `3` (`0001-F2` B-007). No claim names them.

**The fetched kinds (B-075, B-076).** The Feature and item kinds always; the epic kind when the manifest's own text declares `identifiers.epic`. The read is of the manifest as written, before the fill, because the loader still fills the epic grammar from the default whatever the manifest declares (`0001-F5` B-019 as amended is unbuilt; `0001-F5` § 7), so the filled `SpecStructure.Identifiers` cannot say which manifest declared it. `Identifiers` is not changed: epics opt-in (`0001-F5` B-024 to B-035) has no item, and stopping the fill is that item's. When it lands, the fetched kinds read the same fact from `Identifiers` and this read goes. Every manifest that exists today declares the epic grammar, so each one that records a source owes three hashes.

**The address (B-074).** `Address` is `Location` and `FileName` joined with no character between them, the reading B-074's source column records: a `location` with no trailing `/` gives an address that runs the two together, and nothing here rejects or repairs it. It is text, not a `Uri`, so the join is the claim's and not a resolver's. Until item `0093` fetches, B-074's proof is this value on the model; its scenarios run `upgrade` and stay unbound.

**What is not read (OQ-46).** A `spechtSchema` member other than the three, and a `hashes` entry for a kind that is not fetched, are neither rejected nor carried. OQ-46 is open with rejection as its proposed default, and no claim is written; a rejection built on it would be behaviour no claim states.

**What does not change.** No schema file: `.specht/manifest.json`, which the engine embeds as the default manifest, and `src/tool/manifest/v0.1.0/manifest.json` gain no `spechtSchema`. The key is the consumer's and has no default, and a default would make every manifest that leaves it out record a source. So `0001-F4` B-004 holds as it is, `init` writes what it wrote, and the key is schema `0.1.0`'s (OQ-40) by the engine knowing it. Item `0176` added only the record; item `0092` now reads it as described below. A manifest with no `spechtSchema` keeps the embedded path, and `0001-F1` B-004's golden report is unchanged. `0001-F5` B-012 is unchanged in text and test: the keys it rejects are still unknown. `upgrade` (item `0045`) rewrites files only; adding keys is item `0047`'s. When `0047` lands it adds only the keys the pinned version introduces that the manifest lacks, and every present key keeps its value (decision 0001). The pinned version's manifest holds no `spechtSchema`, so none is added and a recorded one survives. What `upgrade` does with a key the pinned version does not define is OQ-14, and it does not reach this key while `0.1.0`, which has it, is the only version shipped.

**The source names its templates (item `0290`, decision 0016, OQ-66; built 2026-10-28).** [`UpstreamSchemaSource`](../../specht/Manifest/UpstreamSchemaSource.cs) gains `Templates`, an init property that defaults to empty, so every existing construction is unchanged. [`UpstreamTemplateFile`](../../specht/Manifest/UpstreamTemplateFile.cs) is one member of `spechtSchema.templates`: its `FileName`, the member's name; its `Address`, the `location` followed by that name, as B-074's (B-131); and its `Hash` (B-125). `SpecManifestParser`'s `SpechtSchema` now takes the pinned `SchemaVersion?` (`versions.Find(number)`), and its private `Templates` reads the members in written order. A value that fails B-073's `IsSha256` (B-126), or a file name the pinned version's `Templates` lacks (B-127), joins `sourceFaults`, so it surfaces as the same `SpechtManifestException` and exit `3`; under a pin the tool does not ship the ship check is skipped, so B-003's fault stays the one reported. The member is read under every pin (owner, 2026-10-28); no version is cut. [`SchemaVersion`](../../specht/Versioning/SchemaVersion.cs)`.Templates`, init and empty by default, is filled by `SchemaVersions` from the engine resources `templates/v<version>/<file>`: `src/specht/specht.csproj` links `src/tool/templates/v*/*.md` as it links the schemas, and `SpechtResources` reads the `templates/v` prefix. The tool keeps its own embedded copy for what `init` and `upgrade` write. Nothing is fetched or verified for a template here (items `0291`, `0292`); a manifest without `templates` checks as before, and `0001-F1` B-004's golden report is unchanged.

**Checking the upstream copy (item `0092`, B-024, B-025, B-028, B-032, B-077 to B-081).** [`SpecSchemasLoader`](../../specht/Model/SpecSchemasLoader.cs) reuses the existing embedded load to read and validate the manifest and select its pinned vocabulary before reading a local schema. With no `SpechtSchema`, it returns that embedded result. With a recording, it reads exactly `UpstreamSchemaSource.Files` through [`FrontmatterSchemaStore`](../../specht/Manifest/FrontmatterSchemaStore.cs), verifies the unchanged bytes through [`UpstreamSchemaCopy`](../../specht/Manifest/UpstreamSchemaCopy.cs), and builds the frontmatter schemas from the verified text. The pinned `SchemaVersion` retains its number and rule ids with only its schema texts replaced. An unfetched epic kind keeps the pinned epic schema; no local epic file is read.

The store reads one file under `.specht/schema/frontmatter/` through the injected `IFileSystem` and returns its bytes or null. It decides no policy. Per the owner's ruling recorded in item `0092`, it is a fifth I/O type until ADR-0010 stage 5 folds it into `SpecManifestStore`; this change does not amend the ADR. The pure verifier compares SHA-256 over the original bytes, before decoding UTF-8 and removing a UTF-8 byte order mark. It collects every absent or mismatched file into one `SpechtManifestException`, naming each path with `/` separators relative to the root. An absent file's fault names `specht init` (OQ-47); whether to name `--schemas` too remains OQ-57. `CheckCommand`'s existing catch prints the fault on stderr with empty stdout and exit `3`. Neither component fetches or writes.

`AddSpechtEngine()` registers both instances alongside the loader. [`SpecSchemaSource`](../../specht/Model/SpecSchemaSource.cs) lives in `Model/`, below the report, so the schemas can carry their source without an upward namespace dependency. `SpechtRunner.Evaluate` copies `SpecSchemas.Source` onto `SpechtReport.SchemaSource`, and `SpecReportDocument.From` copies it into the document. The upstream document says only `upstream`; naming the source address or version is `0001-F3` OQ-6, and B-011's disk provenance is built with item `0043`.

**Locking a version (B-092, B-093, C-17; OQ-52's proposed default).** [`SchemaVersionLock`](../../../.build/SchemaVersionLock.cs) lives in the build assembly; the `LockSchemaVersion` target in [`.build/SpechtBuild.SchemaVersioning.cs`](../../../.build/SpechtBuild.SchemaVersioning.cs) only calls it. `./build.sh LockSchemaVersion` discovers the newest version by parsing `src/tool/schema/v*/` with the .NET `System.Version` type, ordered numerically. It requires exactly three components and an ordinal round trip through `ToString(3)`, preserving the existing canonical `major.minor.patch` spelling. The build references no application project; its tooling reads shipping files directly. `BuildBoundaryUnitTests.TheBuildAssembly_WhenInspected_ShouldNotReferenceApplicationAssemblies` guards the compiled boundary. The committed `src/tool/schema/versions.lock.json` is a JSON object mapping each locked `major.minor.patch` to one lowercase SHA-256 string, with no file list. Existing entries survive an addition. If the newest version already has an entry, the method returns before reading its files or writing the lock, preserving even the lock's whitespace. An idle target emits only NUKE's usual target status. This implements the lock part of OQ-52; the cut target and manifest placement remain open. The committed lock records `0.1.0` and `0.2.0`: `0.1.1`'s folders were deleted by hand and the target then added `0.2.0` (item `0219`, decision 0013), so no shipped version is unlocked and `VerifyTag`'s `RequireAllLockedAsync` passes (`0055-F6` B-013).

The rationale for content identity, schema/tool version independence, the Git commit alternative, and the separation of recording from enforcement are recorded in [ADR-0002](adr/0002-schema-locks-are-independent-of-tool-versions.md).

The digest covers every file recursively under `src/tool/schema/v<version>/`, `src/tool/manifest/v<version>/`, `src/tool/templates/v<version>/` and `docs/rules/v<version>/`. These are the existing shipping trees (reuse: `SchemaVersions.ReadEmbedded`, `InitWriter`, and the engine and host resource declarations); the manifest stays in its current separate tree. Each path is relative to the repository root, uses `/`, and is sorted ordinally. For each file the hash receives its UTF-8 path length as an eight-byte big-endian integer, its path bytes, its content length in the same form, then its unchanged content bytes. Length framing prevents different file boundaries from yielding the same input. No timestamp, absolute root or platform newline conversion enters the hash. A new lock is written to a temporary sibling and moved into place; a recorded digest is never recomputed by locking. Validation of the shipped set against the lock, release gating and cutting a version remain outside this change.

<!-- last written by: implementer, 2026-10-28 (item 0189) -->

**Cutting a version (item `0189`, B-094 to B-100; OQ-52's proposed default; built).** `SchemaVersionCut` sits in [`.build/SchemaVersionCut.cs`](../../../.build/SchemaVersionCut.cs), in the build assembly beside the lock: `public sealed class SchemaVersionCut(IFileSystem fileSystem, string root)` with one member, `public async Task CutAsync(string version)`, failing by throwing `InvalidOperationException`, as `RequireAllLockedAsync` does. The `CutSchemaVersion` target in [`.build/SpechtBuild.SchemaVersioning.cs`](../../../.build/SpechtBuild.SchemaVersioning.cs) only calls it: `./build.sh CutSchemaVersion --cut-version 0.3.0`. The version is a `[Parameter]` field `private readonly string? CutVersion;`, declared as `SpechtBuild.cs` declares its parameters, and the target passes `CutVersion ?? string.Empty`, so an absent value meets B-095's malformed refusal in code a test reaches, not in NUKE's `Requires`. A test constructs the class over a `MockFileSystem` or a real root under `.artifacts/`, as the lock's tests do; the target holds no logic.

**Shared with the lock, not duplicated.** The cut constructs `new SchemaVersionLock(fileSystem, root)` and uses three of its members. `ReadVersions` and `ShippingTrees` go from `private` to `internal`, bodies unchanged. The predicate inside `ReadVersions` - three components and an ordinal round trip through `ToString(3)` - is extracted as `internal static bool TryParseVersion(string text, [NotNullWhen(true)] out Version? version)`, and `ReadVersions` still throws its own message when it returns false. No test reads an `internal` member and no `InternalsVisibleTo` is added, so B-089 to B-093 stay green on unchanged tests. The parse is pure and holds no state; a second class holding the trees would be a seam with one caller.

**Every refusal before the first write.** `CutAsync` reads and plans fully, then writes.

| Step                                                                                 | Refuses with, paths repository-relative                                                                                                                                           | Claim                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- |
| 1. `RequireAllLockedAsync()`                                                         | its existing `Shipped schema versions are not locked in src/tool/schema/versions.lock.json: 0.2.0.`                                                                               | B-094                       |
| 2. `TryParseVersion(version)`                                                        | `The cut version '2' is not major.minor.patch.`                                                                                                                                   | B-095                       |
| 3. Compare with `ReadVersions().Max()`                                               | `The cut version 0.2.0 is not higher than the newest shipped schema version 0.2.0.`                                                                                               | B-095                       |
| 4. For each of `ShippingTrees` holding `v<newest>`, `<tree>/v<new>` is absent        | `docs/rules/v0.3.0/ already exists.`                                                                                                                                              | none; a folder left by hand |
| 5. Read every file under each `<tree>/v<newest>/`, recursively, and splice in memory | `src/tool/schema/v0.2.1/frontmatter/task.schema.json does not state its $id exactly once.` or `docs/rules/v0.2.1/SPEC002.md does not give its schema version in exactly one row.` | B-097, B-098                |

When two refusals apply, the first in that order is reported; no claim orders them. Step 3 compares `System.Version`, which orders numerically, so `0.10.0` is higher than `0.9.0`. Step 4 is unclaimed, so untested; `Directory.Move` refuses an existing destination anyway, and the plan only moves that refusal before the first write. A tree with no `v<newest>` folder gets no `v<new>` folder (B-096).

**Staging.** Each tree's planned files are written under a staging sibling `<tree>/.v<new>.<guid>.tmp/`, all four trees before any move; each staging folder is then moved to `<tree>/v<new>` with `Directory.Move`, a rename within one directory. A failure while staging leaves no `v<new>` folder; a failure between moves deletes the `v<new>` folders this run moved and rethrows; `finally` deletes every staging folder that remains. A refused or failed cut leaves the tree as it was, so B-094 and B-095's "creates no file" holds on every path. The leading dot keeps a staging folder outside the lock's `v*` discovery and the project files' `v*` globs. A killed process can leave a staging or moved folder, which `git status` shows untracked; deleting it is the recovery. Whether a transient staging file counts against B-100 is the reading OQ-63 asks of `upgrade`; the B-100 scenario compares the tree before and after the call.

**Two splices, bytes only (B-097 to B-099).** `private static byte[] Splice(byte[] bytes, byte[] token, byte[] replacement, string refusal)` replaces `token` in the bytes it is given and fails with step 5's message, passed as `refusal`, unless it occurs exactly once; `Rewrite` chooses the token by tree and path. Every token is ASCII, and an ASCII byte never occurs inside a UTF-8 multi-byte sequence, so a match falls on character boundaries. The file is never decoded, so its byte order mark, line endings, final newline and every other byte are the newest's.

- Each `*.schema.json` directly under `src/tool/schema/v<newest>/frontmatter/`: the token is `https://github.com/rlittlesii/specht/schema/v<newest>/frontmatter/`, the replacement the same with `v<new>` (B-087). Today it occurs once per file, in `$id`; a `$ref` carrying the absolute address would refuse the cut rather than be rewritten.
- Each `*.md` directly under `docs/rules/v<newest>/`: the token is `<newest>`, spaces included, searched only within the one line that begins `| Schema version `, and replaced by `<new>` (`0001-F3` B-033). The cell's padding is left as it is.
- Every other file - the templates, the manifest, anything else in those trees - is copied byte for byte.

**What the cut leaves to the maintainer.** Recorded, not solved:

- A version string of another length, `0.9.0` to `0.10.0`, misaligns each rule page's metadata table, because B-099 leaves the padding. `./build.sh Format` checks `**/*.md` with Prettier and flags those pages; the maintainer formats them after the cut.
- The new version's rule vocabulary is written by hand in `SchemaVersions.Shipped` (§ 5 row 25). Until that arm exists the engine refuses the embedded `schema/v<new>/` on first read, so the cut and the arm land in one change.
- This repository's live `.specht/` and its pin move by hand (OQ-56's proposed default), so `0001-F4` B-004's comparison fails until they do.
- The manifest keeps the newest's `schemaVersion` (OQ-67's proposed default (a)): `init` after a cut writes the previous pin, and `InitCommand.Integration.Tests`' newest-pin test fails, until the maintainer edits `src/tool/manifest/v<new>/manifest.json`. Reading (b) adds one splice, the token `"schemaVersion": "<newest>"` in that one file.
- Spike [`0220`](../../../.issue/0220-rule-page-versioning-spike.yml) may later replace the per-version rule page snapshot. The cut is built on today's snapshot; the spike states that 0189 does not wait on it, and if its recommendation replaces the snapshot, the copy of `docs/rules/` here is redone with it.

<!-- last written by: implementer, 2026-10-29 (item `0044`, built) -->

**Verifying the shipped versions (item `0044`, B-004, B-040, B-066, B-088 to B-091; built).** Two members in the build assembly, beside the lock and the cut, each return an `IReadOnlyList` of findings and throw for no fault, so one call reports every fault. No target calls either; the two real-tree tests § 8 names call them on every build. A finding is a record of strings and a fault enum, one type per file (RSA2008, RSA2009): [`SchemaVersionLockFinding`](../../../.build/SchemaVersionLockFinding.cs) with [`SchemaVersionLockFault`](../../../.build/SchemaVersionLockFault.cs), and [`SchemaVersionNumberingFinding`](../../../.build/SchemaVersionNumberingFinding.cs) with [`SchemaVersionNumberingFault`](../../../.build/SchemaVersionNumberingFault.cs).

[`SchemaVersionLock.VerifyAsync`](../../../.build/SchemaVersionLock.cs) reads the lock through a private `ReadEntriesAsync`, extracted from `RequireAllLockedAsync`, which now calls it too: a missing lock is empty, and a lock that is not an object throws as before. "Shipped" is `ReadVersions`, the `src/tool/schema/v*/` folders.

| Input                                                       | Finding                         | Claim |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------- | ----- |
| A lock entry that ships, whose `ComputeDigestAsync` differs | `DigestMismatch`                | B-089 |
| A lock entry with no folder, `1.0.0` or higher              | `LockedVersionNotShipped`       | B-004 |
| A lock entry with no folder, below `1.0.0`                  | none, and no digest is computed | B-004 |
| Each unlocked shipped version, when more than one is        | `MoreThanOneUnlocked`           | B-090 |
| An unlocked shipped version below the highest shipped one   | `UnlockedNotNewest`             | B-091 |

Passing over an entry below `1.0.0` is OQ-55 reading (a), the owner's ruling of 2026-10-29: before `1.0.0` a later tool may stop shipping a `0.x.y` (C-1, decision 0007). A lock key that is not `major.minor.patch` has no folder and is passed over; no claim covers it. Lock entries come first, in ordinal key order, then the unlocked versions ascending; no claim orders them.

[`SchemaVersionNumbering`](../../../.build/SchemaVersionNumbering.cs) is `public sealed class SchemaVersionNumbering(IFileSystem fileSystem, string root)` with the one member `VerifyAsync`. It takes the shipped versions from `new SchemaVersionLock(fileSystem, root).ReadVersions()` and the trees from `SchemaVersionLock.ShippingTrees`, as the cut does, and walks each version against the next higher by `System.Version` precedence. A version's rule ids are the `*.md` names directly under `docs/rules/v<version>/`, since the build references no application project.

| Neighbours                                                                                                          | Finding                             | Claim |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ----- |
| A rule id the higher holds and the lower lacks; the lower `1.0.0` or higher; the higher's major not greater         | `NewRuleWithoutHigherMajor`         | B-040 |
| A rule id the higher holds and the lower lacks; the higher below `1.0.0`; its minor not greater                     | `NewRuleWithoutHigherMinor`         | B-066 |
| The same rule ids; every schema and manifest file equal; some template or rule page different; major or minor moved | `TemplateOrRulePageChangeNotAPatch` | B-088 |

For B-088 each file of both versions is read as text, split on `\n`, and the line in which it states its own version is dropped before comparing: in `src/tool/schema/` a line holding `"$id"` and `/v<version>/` (B-087), in `src/tool/manifest/` a line holding `"schemaVersion"` and `"<version>"`, in `docs/rules/` a line beginning `| Schema version ` and holding the version with a space either side (`0001-F3` B-033). Dropping the line rather than substituting the number keeps the cell padding, which a number of another length changes, out of the comparison, and treats `0.1.0`'s manifest, which states no `schemaVersion`, alike. A file on one side only is a difference: in the schema or manifest tree it rules B-088 out; among the templates or rule pages it is a template or rule-page change. Neighbours identical once the statements are dropped are not reported, since they differ in nothing; § 8 lists that case as unclaimed. A pair either side of `1.0.0` with a new rule id is neither B-040's nor B-066's and is not reported.

### Upgrade to the pin

<!-- last written by: implementer, 2026-10-28 (item `0047`, built) -->

**Designed 2026-10-24 and built for item `0045`; item `0047`'s extension
designed and built 2026-10-28.** `specht upgrade` makes the
pinned version's schema and template files on disk the shipped bytes. It reads
the pin and never writes it (B-052, C-13). Items `0046`, `0047`, `0048`,
`0093`, `0094` and `0131` extend this path; none of them replaces it. The
command keeps the name `upgrade` while OQ-12 is open.

**The slice.** `src/tool/Features/Upgrade/` holds three files, as `Init/` does:

| File                     | Holds                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `UpgradeCommand.cs`      | The command and its nested `Settings`, `--root` only. It calls `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade(Path.GetFullPath(root), token)`, prints, and folds to an exit code |
| `UpgradeWriter.cs`       | The comparison and the rewrite                                                                                                                           |
| `UpgradeRegistration.cs` | `AddUpgrade(IServiceCollection)` and `AddUpgrade(IConfigurator)`, the `"upgrade"` command and its description                                            |

`Program.cs` gains `services.AddUpgrade();` after `services.AddInit();` and
`.AddUpgrade()` in the `config.AddInit().AddSchema()` chain, until item `0144`
moves the list into the factory (ADR-0007). Host tests compose through the same
two extensions. The writer lives in the host slice, not beside
`SchemaVersionManager`: the templates are embedded only in the tool assembly
(`src/tool/tool.csproj`), and the engine holds the schemas only as decoded text
(`SpechtResources.Texts`), which cannot answer a byte comparison.

**The shipped files are one object with two callers.** `InitWriter.Selected`
already maps `schema/v<version>/<path>` and `templates/v<version>/<path>` to
`.specht/schema/<path>` and `.specht/templates/<path>` through its `Shipped()`
pattern. That mapping moves to `ShippedFiles` at `src/tool/Features/ShippedFiles.cs`,
the `Features/` root, because a second slice now reads it. It wraps the
dictionary `InitWriter.ShippingCopy(Assembly)` already reads. `At(version)`
returns that version's root-relative paths and bytes in ordinal order.
`InitWriter` keeps its constructor and filters `At` by part. `UpgradeWriter`
takes the frontmatter and template entries, and from item `0047` the manifest
entry as the source of the keys it adds, never as bytes to write. No new
static member and no second resource read are added.

**What is compared.** `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` reads the manifest through
`SpecSchemasLoader.LoadManifest`, the check's read, and the pin through
`SpecManifestParser.PinnedVersion`. Under that pin, for each shipped file:

| On disk                    | Outcome                            | Claim                         |
| -------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ----------------------------- |
| Absent                     | Not written; the part stays absent | B-139; decision 0018          |
| Bytes equal to the shipped | Current                            | B-006                         |
| Any byte differs           | Rewritten with the shipped bytes   | B-005, older or newer version |

The comparison is byte-exact, `ReadOnlySpan<byte>.SequenceEqual`, with no
decoding, no line-ending normalisation and no `$id` reading, the same identity
the version lock uses (C-17). A hand edit, a version's change and a CRLF
checkout all differ. The epic schema follows its presence. `init` writes it
only where its rule allows, so presence never adds an epic schema `init` would
not have written. File names are the shipped names, as `init` writes them under
the embedded source; `frontmatterSchemas` names apply to the disk and upstream
paths that `0094` and `0093` own.

**Absent files stay absent (B-139; decision 0018, OQ-51 (a)).** A part the
consumer never asked `init` for stays absent, and `init --schemas` or
`init --templates` adds it.

**What is never touched.** A file in either folder that the pinned version does
not ship, whether an older version's or the consumer's, is not read (B-063,
C-2). With `SpecStructure.SchemaSource` set to `Disk`, or a recorded
`SpecStructure.SpechtSchema`, no frontmatter schema is read or written, the
templates are still compared, and no network type is reached (B-029, B-067,
C-8). Item `0094` adds the skip listing. Item `0093` replaced the upstream
branch on 2026-10-29; "Upgrade fetches the recorded source" below states the
replacement.
`upgrade` takes no `--schema-source` option; whether it gains one belongs to
`0094`. Until item `0130` adds the key, every shipped template is compared.
Item `0131` removes the named ones before the comparison (B-060).

**The plan, then the writes.** Every read and comparison finishes before the
first write, so a failed read writes nothing. Rewrites go in ordinal path order
through `SpecManifestStore.ReplaceConfigurationFile(root, relative, bytes,
token)`. That is the manifest's staged sibling and atomic replace,
generalised; `WriteManifest` becomes a call to it with `RelativePath`. It
refuses a relative path that is not under `FrontmatterSchemaFolder`, a new
`TemplateFolder` constant (`.specht/templates`), or `RelativePath` itself. That
puts B-007's boundary in the one method that writes. The staging sibling exists
only during one replace and is removed on success and on failure, so a
before-and-after snapshot (B-007, B-063) sees only rewritten files.
`ReadConfigurationFile(root, relative)` returns bytes or null for the
comparison; `ReadFrontmatterSchema` remains for the loader. A failure partway
through leaves earlier files rewritten, and a second run completes the rest.

**The result and the output.** `UpgradeResult(SemanticVersion Pinned,
IReadOnlyList<string> Rewritten)` carries root-relative `/` paths (C-7). With
nothing rewritten, `UpgradeCommand` prints
`.specht/ is current at schemaVersion <pin>` and exits `0` (B-006). After a
rewrite it exits `0` (B-005) and prints nothing in item `0045`. Item `0047`
adds the missing manifest keys to the plan (B-016) and the listing to standard
output (B-017); "Manifest keys and the listing" below states both.

**Manifest keys and the listing (item `0047`).** Designed and built 2026-10-28. B-016
names the keys, B-138 keeps the epic grammar out of them, B-017 and OQ-24 the
output, B-059 the exit code, and OQ-64's proposed default (a) extends B-006's
judgement to a missing key.

_Which keys._ The keys a version defines are read from the
`.specht/manifest.json` entry of `ShippedFiles.At(pinned)`, the bytes `init`
writes for that version. The engine's per-version default manifests come from
the same `src/tool/manifest/v<version>/manifest.json` files (`specht.csproj`),
so `upgrade`'s keys and the parser's omission defaults have one source. A key is defined when it is a top-level member of that manifest, is
neither `schemaVersion` nor `identifiers`, and does not begin with `$`.
`identifiers` is excluded by the owner's ruling on OQ-68: every shipped
manifest's `identifiers` declares the epic grammar, the one value the check
never fills from the default manifest (`0001-F5` decision 0004), so copying it
would opt a manifest that lacks it into epics (B-138). A key is present when the
consumer's manifest holds a top-level member of that name, ordinal, whatever
its value, `null` included. Only top-level keys are compared; nothing inside a
present value is read or added.

_Who edits._ A new engine class, `ManifestKeyEditor(ManifestPinEditor manifest)`
in `src/specht/Manifest/ManifestKeyEditor.cs`, registered beside
`ManifestPinEditor` in `SpechtEngineServiceCollectionExtensions`. It follows
`ManifestRuleEditor`: a sibling that borrows the pin editor's `Format` and
`OriginalOffset`, so the BOM, encoding and offset arithmetic stay in one place.
The method is not added to `ManifestPinEditor`, whose contract is "only the pin's
bytes" (C-13). It lives in the engine because those two members are `internal`.
It is a concrete class with no interface (ADR-0014).

```
public byte[] AddMissing(byte[] bytes, byte[] shipped)
```

`bytes` is the consumer's manifest as read; `shipped` is the shipped manifest,
UTF-8 without a BOM, as every shipped manifest is. It returns `bytes` itself
when no defined key is missing. Otherwise it returns a new array in which every
original byte is unchanged, the BOM and encoding included, and one span is
inserted:

- **Where.** After the end of the root object's last member, before the
  whitespace and the closing brace that follow it. In an object with no members
  the span goes directly after `{`. The insertion is a byte splice, as
  `ManifestPinEditor.Change` adds a missing pin. Nothing is re-serialised.
- **What.** For each missing key, in the shipped manifest's member order: `,`
  (omitted only for the first key in an object with no members), then `\n` and
  two spaces, then the shipped manifest's own text for that member. That text
  starts at the opening quote of the name and ends at the end of the value. It is
  copied verbatim and transcoded into the consumer's encoding.
- **Why verbatim.** Re-serialising through `System.Text.Json` would escape
  characters the shipped text writes plainly. For example, the default encoder
  writes `+` in the identifier patterns as `+`. A copy gives one form for
  every key, the same as `init` writes. The two-space indent and `\n` are the
  shipped manifest's own formatting, so a manifest `init` wrote gains members in
  its own layout. A manifest in another layout, CRLF or minified, gains them in
  the shipped layout. No claim governs formatting, and the check reads both the
  same way.

For example, `{\n  "schemaVersion": "0.2.0"\n}\n` against version `0.2.0`
becomes the shipped `0.2.0` manifest's bytes minus its `$comment` member and
its `identifiers` member.

Walking the consumer's bytes uses `Utf8JsonReader` with the defaults `Change`
uses. A root that is not an object throws `SpechtManifestException`
(`.specht/manifest.json: expected a manifest object.`), as `Change` does. The
loader rejects such a manifest first, so no test reaches that branch through
`upgrade`.

_The pin and the record._ `schemaVersion` is never added and no present member
is touched. A manifest with no pin therefore keeps none (B-052, C-13), and a
recorded `spechtSchema` keeps every byte (B-034, C-10). The manifest's keys are
added under every schema source and with or without a recorded upstream source;
B-016 sets no condition on either. The source is why `identifiers` is not among
them: under `schemaSource: disk`, or beside a `spechtSchema` that records no
epic hash, a manifest that gained the epic grammar would fail its next check
with exit `3` for want of an epic schema the consumer never chose (OQ-68). With
it excluded, a manifest that declared no epic grammar declares none after the
upgrade under any source (B-138), and one that holds `identifiers` keeps it
byte for byte.

_The plan._ `UpgradeWriter` gains a `ManifestKeyEditor keys` constructor
parameter and stops filtering out the manifest entry. For each entry of
`At(pinned)`, still in ordinal order and still subject to the frontmatter
filter, it reads the present bytes through `ReadConfigurationFile`, and an
absent file stays out of the plan. The planned bytes are the shipped bytes,
except for `.specht/manifest.json`, whose planned bytes are
`keys.AddMissing(present, shipped)`. An entry joins the plan when its planned
bytes differ from the present ones (`SequenceEqual`). That is one rule for all
three kinds of file. All reads and edits finish before the first write. The
writes then go through the one loop and the one
`ReplaceConfigurationFile` call, which already permits `RelativePath`. It is
the call `WriteManifest` makes. Ordinally `.specht/manifest.json` sorts before
`.specht/schema/` and `.specht/templates/`, so the manifest is planned, written
and listed first. A write failure keeps the existing mapping to
`SpechtManifestException` naming the root-relative file, and a second run
completes the rest.

_The result._ `UpgradeResult` is unchanged. `Rewritten` holds
`.specht/manifest.json` exactly when a key was added. Empty `Rewritten` now
means that no file differs and no key is missing (B-006 under OQ-64 (a)).

_The output._ `UpgradeCommand` writes plain lines through
`console.Profile.Out.Writer`, with no markup, so a `[` in a path is printed as
typed. With `Rewritten` empty it prints only the existing line and exits `0`:

```
.specht/ is current at schemaVersion <pin>
```

Otherwise it prints the version line, then one line per entry of `Rewritten`
in its order, and exits `0` (B-005, B-017, B-059):

```
.specht/ rewritten to schemaVersion <pin>
rewritten <root-relative path>
```

`<pin>` is `SemanticVersion.ToString()`, and each path is root-relative with
`/` (C-7). "Rewritten" fits a move up or down and is `init`'s
`written`/`skipped` form. No diff, no key name and no previous version is
printed (B-017, OQ-24). If OQ-21 adds the previous version, only the version
line changes. Skip lines from items `0094` and `0131` (B-030, B-061) are theirs
to place. The command's help description in `UpgradeRegistration` and
`docs/cli.md` § `specht upgrade` name the manifest keys and both output forms.

_Fixtures this changes._ Item `0045`'s writer, command and acceptance fixtures
pin `0.1.0` with a manifest of only `schemaVersion`. After `0047` that manifest
lacks every `0.1.0` key, so a fixture that expects "current" or an empty
`Rewritten` needs a manifest that holds every key (OQ-64 (a)), or it now
expects `.specht/manifest.json` first in the list.

_ADR-0014._ One new concrete class with one caller now. The class is earned
because the edit belongs beside the other two byte editors, not in the host.
It adds no abstraction, no static and no second resource read. No ADR is
recorded: the insertion form is a design choice within B-016, not a repository
rule.

**Failures before `0046` and `0048`.** The loader and parser throw the check's
exceptions. `UpgradeCommand` catches them the way `CheckCommand` does:
`SpechtRootNotFoundException` and `SpechtManifestNotFoundException` give `2`;
`SpechtManifestUnreadableException` and `SpechtManifestException` give `3`, an
unshipped or malformed pin included. Each writes one `specht:` line to standard
error, and none writes a file. Items `0046` (B-018 to B-020) and `0048` (B-021)
prove those paths and own any divergence from the check. An `IOException` or
`UnauthorizedAccessException` while writing becomes a `SpechtManifestException`
naming the root-relative file, as `SchemaVersionManager.Pin` already maps one,
so no absolute path reaches the host's handler (brief § 9). Exit `3` there is
the pin command's precedent; no claim names it.

**ADR-0014.** The design uses the existing store, parser, loader and
hand-written composition: no mediator, no CQRS contract, no new abstraction and
no architecture migration. `ShippedFiles` has two real callers. The store
methods extend `SpecManifestStore` and replace nothing. No ADR is recorded,
because no repository rule changes.

### The consumer's templates key (0130)

<!-- last written by: implementer, 2026-10-24 (item `0130`, ratified 2026-10-24) -->

**Ratified 2026-10-24 by the repository owner under OQ-1 ([decision 0017](decisions/0017-the-consumers-templates-are-named-in-a-consumertemplates-array.md)).**

**The key.** `consumerTemplates`, a top-level array of strings. Each entry is a
template file name as the pinned version ships it, relative to
`.specht/templates/`: `["feature.md", "adr.md"]`. It mirrors the values of
`frontmatterSchemas` (file names, not paths) and the array keys beside it
(`exclusions`, `taskFiles`). Absent and `[]` mean the same: no template is the
consumer's, which is every manifest today.

**Rejected names and forms.** `templates`: it collides in the reader's head
with the `templates` hash member of `spechtSchema` (decisions 0008 and 0016, OQ-65).
A map keyed by kind: `adr`, `decision` and `lesson` have no kind, and nothing
configures a template beyond its name. An object per template: the same, and it
would invite the per-template settings no claim asks for. A boolean switch: the
all-or-nothing form decision 0006 rejected.

**Part of `0.1.0`.** `consumerTemplates` joins `SpecManifestParser.KnownKeys`
in `src/specht/Manifest/SpecManifestParser.cs`, which every pin shares, so
`0001-F5` B-012 stops rejecting it at `0.1.0` and later without the version
gates `claim`, `schemaSource` and the matrix keys carry. No embedded manifest,
no frontmatter schema and no schema version changes: the key has no default,
so an embedded manifest holds none, and the lock digest and
`this repository's .specht/manifest.json` are untouched. A bare `[]` is not
written by `init` or `upgrade` (`0047` adds only keys with a default).

**Loader and model.** `SpecStructure` (`src/specht/Manifest/SpecStructure.cs`)
gains `ConsumerTemplates`, an `IReadOnlyList<string>` that defaults to empty,
set by `SpecManifestParser.Parse` through the existing `Strings` helper, which
already faults a value that is not an array of strings. No further form check is
added: an empty entry, a path separator or `..` in an entry is accepted, because
no claim states a rule for it and nothing reads the entry as a path. The parser
does not compare an entry with the pinned version's templates: whether a name
the version does not ship is invalid is OQ-27, owner's and open, so the parser
accepts such a name and nothing acts on it. A repeated entry is accepted.
The check reads `ConsumerTemplates` nowhere (B-065, § 5 row 15), so its report
is the one the same tree gets without the key.

**How `0131` reads it.** `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` already holds the
`SpecStructure` from `loader.LoadManifest(root)`. `0131` removes from the shipped
template entries every file name in `structure.ConsumerTemplates` before the
comparison, records each as skipped for `0047`'s listing (B-061), and leaves the
upstream paths of `0093` and `0291` the same filter. No second manifest read and
no new key are needed. `0131` also decides, with OQ-27 still open, nothing
about a name the pin does not ship.

### Upgrade fetches the recorded source

<!-- last written by: implementer, 2026-10-29 (items `0093` and `0291`, built) -->

**Designed 2026-10-24 and built 2026-10-29 for item `0093`.** Under a recorded
`SpecStructure.SpechtSchema`, `upgrade` brings each recorded kind's local copy
to the recorded hash, fetching only a copy that differs (B-026, B-033). It
replaces item `0045`'s upstream skip in `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` and reuses the
fetch and hash check item `0095` built for `init`. No new domain type is added.

**Which branch runs.** The manifest read is `0045`'s:

| Manifest                                      | Frontmatter schemas                        | Network       |
| --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ------------- |
| No record, embedded source                    | Compared to the pinned version's (`0045`)  | None (B-067)  |
| No record, on-disk source                     | Not read or written; `0094` lists the skip | None (B-067)  |
| A record, implied or explicit on-disk source  | This subsection                            | Only to fetch |
| A record and an explicit `embedded` selection | Rejected at parse, exit `3` (B-036)        | None          |

A record keeps its hashes under the on-disk source (B-024, B-036, decision
0011), so an explicit `"schemaSource": "disk"` beside a record is the third
row. The fourth is `SpecManifestParser.Parse`'s existing contradiction fault,
reached through `LoadManifest`. `upgrade` has no `--schema-source` option, so
the command-line half of B-036 does not arise.

**The local comparison.** For each `UpstreamSchemaFile` in
`UpstreamSchemaSource.Files`, which already holds the epic kind only under the
declared grammar (B-075, B-076), the writer reads
`SpecManifestStore.ReadConfigurationFile(root, FrontmatterSchemaFolder + "/" +
file.FileName)`. That read goes through the store's `Permitted` path check, so
a `frontmatterSchemas` name that leaves the folder is a
`SpechtManifestException` and exit `3` before any network call; `InitWriter`'s
own check is not copied.

| Local copy              | Outcome                                     | Claim             |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | ----------------- |
| Absent                  | Not fetched, not written; stays absent      | B-140             |
| SHA-256 equals the hash | Current; no network call                    | B-033             |
| SHA-256 differs         | Fetched; written only if the fetch verifies | B-026, B-082, C-9 |

An absent copy is not "a local copy whose content hash differs" (B-026), and
the check already names `specht init` as the command that fetches it (B-079,
OQ-47). `init --schemas` beside the existing manifest fetches every absent
recorded kind today. This keeps `0045`'s "absent stays absent", as decision
0018 rules (OQ-51 (a)).

The hash is compared through one new member,
`UpstreamSchemaFile.Matches(byte[] bytes)`: `sha256:` and the lowercase
hexadecimal SHA-256 of the bytes, equal by ordinal to `Hash`.
`UpstreamSchemaFetcher.Fetch` for a schema, `UpstreamSchemaCopy.Verify` and
the writer call it, so C-9's identity for a schema has one statement and three
callers. Item `0291` gives `UpstreamTemplateFile` the same member, below. `upgrade` does not call `UpstreamSchemaCopy`: it
rejects an absent kind and returns decoded text, and `upgrade` needs neither.

**The plan, then the fetches, then the writes.** `Upgrade` builds its plan in
three passes, so nothing is written until everything that can fail has run:

1. The local reads and comparisons: `0045`'s template comparison, and the
   recorded kinds above. No network type is reached in this pass.
2. One `UpstreamSchemaFetcher.Fetch(file, token)` for each differing kind, in
   `Files` order. The fetcher returns bytes only after their hash matches, so a
   returned file is a verified one. The first `SpechtUpstreamSchemaException`
   ends the run.
3. The writes, templates and fetched schemas together in ordinal path order,
   through `SpecManifestStore.ReplaceConfigurationFile`, each schema under
   `FrontmatterSchemaFolder + "/" + file.FileName` (B-082, C-2).

A mismatch or an unreachable source in pass 2 therefore writes no schema and
no template (B-027, B-035), as decision 0018 rules OQ-44 for `upgrade` and
`0001-F4` decision 0005 rules it for `init`. A write failure in pass 3 keeps `0045`'s
mapping and OQ-62's proposed default (a).

**What is printed.** `UpstreamSchemaFetcher` names the source by its address,
the recorded `location` followed by the file name, with user information
removed: `upstream schema source '<address>' could not be reached.`, or that it
`does not match its recorded SHA-256.` The address is a URL the consumer
recorded, not a path the tool derives, so C-7 and brief § 9 do not reach it; no
transport detail and no local path is copied in. `UpgradeCommand` adds
`SpechtUpstreamSchemaException` to its exit-`3` catch beside
`SpechtManifestException` and `SpechtManifestUnreadableException`, as
`InitCommand` does, and writes one `specht:` line to standard error. A source
that answers `401` or `404` fails `GetByteArrayAsync` as an unreachable one
does, so a refusal gives the same line and exit `3` (B-035), as decision 0018
rules OQ-45 for `upgrade`, `init` already has it (`0001-F4` B-016) and B-133
states it for a template.

**The record is never written (B-034, C-10).** The writer reads the manifest
and writes no byte of the record. Item `0047`'s missing-key fill (B-016) adds
only keys the pinned version defines and never touches a present member;
`spechtSchema` is not one, so the fill never adds or changes a record.

**No network without cause (B-067, B-033, C-8).** The fetcher is called only
from pass 2, which runs only for a recorded kind whose present copy differs.
Tests compose the real writer and fetcher over a synthetic `HttpMessageHandler`
(ADR-0013) that records each request and fails any it receives; B-067 and
B-033 assert it recorded none and the run exits `0`.

**No credential (B-083, C-15 as amended).** The fetcher's `HttpClient` is the
one `init` uses: no credentials, no default or proxy credentials, no cookies
and no redirects, and the fetcher removes user information from the address.
Nothing reads the environment, a credential store or the manifest for a token,
and no header is set. B-083's test sets a token in the process environment and
asserts the recorded request carries no `Authorization` header and no user
information. The next version's credential, sent only to the recorded host
(decision 0016 call 2), is not built.

**Composition.** The client and the fetcher are registered once, for both
slices. `InitRegistration.AddInit` holds both today; they move to
`src/tool/Features/UpstreamFetchRegistration.cs`, the `Features/` root as for
`ShippedFiles`, as `AddUpstreamSchemaFetch(this IServiceCollection)` with
`TryAddSingleton` for each. `AddInit` and `AddUpgrade` both call it, so
composing either or both registers one client, and a test that registers its
own `HttpClient` first keeps it. `UpgradeWriter` gains a required
`UpstreamSchemaFetcher` constructor parameter, resolved by the container; it
has no embedded-only caller to keep it optional for. The assembly-reference
test that allows the handler's configuration only inside `AddInit`
(`EngineAssembly.AllowedNetworkReference`) follows the move to the new site.
No new static state, no interface over the fetcher (the handler is the seam),
no mediator and no CQRS contract (ADR-0014). No ADR is recorded: ADR-0013
already rules where the fetch sits and how its client is configured.

**Templates under a record, built 2026-10-29 for item `0291`.** Each
`UpstreamTemplateFile` in `UpstreamSchemaSource.Templates` (B-125, item `0290`)
names a template under `SpecManifestStore.TemplateFolder` that the record
hashes. `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` takes every such file name out of the pinned
version's comparison (B-062 as amended), then treats each one the manifest does
not name in `SpecStructure.ConsumerTemplates` as it treats a recorded kind:

| Hashed template              | Outcome                                     | Claim               |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
| Named in `consumerTemplates` | Not read, fetched or written                | B-135 (item `0131`) |
| Absent                       | Not fetched, not written; stays absent      | B-141               |
| SHA-256 equals the hash      | Current; no network call                    | B-134, B-136        |
| SHA-256 differs              | Fetched; written only if the fetch verifies | B-130, B-131, C-9   |

A template the record does not hash is compared to the pinned version's, as in
`0045`. The comparison is
[`UpstreamTemplateFile.Matches`](../../specht/Manifest/UpstreamTemplateFile.cs),
the same statement as `UpstreamSchemaFile.Matches`, and
`UpstreamSchemaFetcher`'s template overload now calls it, so the fetcher no
longer states the template identity itself. The two records share nothing else,
so the one-line member is repeated rather than moved to a base record or a
static helper.

The three passes keep their order. Pass 1 also reads each hashed template's
copy; pass 2 fetches every differing schema in `Files` order and then every
differing template in `Templates` order, each adding a `ShippedFile` under
`TemplateFolder + "/" + file.FileName`; pass 3 writes the whole plan in ordinal
path order. A refused template fetch therefore leaves the tree, the manifest
included, as it was (B-132, B-133), and the line on standard error is the
fetcher's, naming the address without user information. A fetched template is
one more entry of `UpgradeResult.Rewritten` (B-017). No credential is sent: the
template request goes through the same private fetch and client as a schema's
(B-137, C-15).

**As built, 2026-10-29.** The design above holds, with these deltas:

| Where                                                                            | As built                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `UpstreamSchemaFile.Matches` (`src/specht/Manifest/UpstreamSchemaFile.cs`)       | Built as designed. `UpstreamSchemaCopy.Verify`, the fetcher's schema overload and `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` call it                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `UpstreamSchemaFetcher` (`src/specht/Manifest/UpstreamSchemaFetcher.cs`)         | Its shared private fetch takes the match as a predicate, not the hash text. The template overload, added by item `0292` after this design, passed its own SHA-256 comparison until item `0291` gave `UpstreamTemplateFile` its `Matches`; it now passes that member                                               |
| `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` (`src/tool/Features/Upgrade/UpgradeWriter.cs`)           | Pass 1 builds `0045`'s and `0047`'s plan, then reads each recorded kind's copy. Pass 2 fetches each differing kind in `Files` order and adds it to the plan as a `ShippedFile`. One ordinal sort by path precedes pass 3, so `UpgradeResult.Rewritten` stays in ordinal order with the fetched schemas among them |
| `UpgradeCommand` (`src/tool/Features/Upgrade/UpgradeCommand.cs`)                 | `SpechtUpstreamSchemaException` joins the exit-`3` catch                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `UpstreamFetchRegistration` (`src/tool/Features/UpstreamFetchRegistration.cs`)   | `AddUpstreamSchemaFetch` registers the client and the fetcher with `TryAddSingleton`. `AddInit` and `AddUpgrade` call it; `InitWriter`'s registration is unchanged                                                                                                                                                |
| `EngineAssembly.AllowedNetworkReference` (`test/tests/Shared/EngineAssembly.cs`) | The handler-configuration site moves to `Specht.Tool.Features.UpstreamFetchRegistration+<>c.<AddUpstreamSchemaFetch>`; the allowed members are unchanged                                                                                                                                                          |

No ADR, no new static state, no interface, no mediator (ADR-0013, ADR-0014).
The engine's existing stores, parser and fetcher are the implementation basis.

**What is not here.** The listing is `0047`'s (B-017): a fetched schema is one
more entry of `UpgradeResult.Rewritten`, so it is listed as a rewritten file.
The on-disk skip and its listing are `0094`'s. The input failures are `0046`'s
and `0048`'s. Moving the recorded version is the consumer's (C-10).

## 8. Testing Strategy

### Schema command proof

<!-- last written by: test-writer, 2026-10-14 -->

Real-process listing tests cover stdout, the mark, recovery stderr and exit
codes. `SchemaListUnitTests` covers optional manifests and rejected inputs;
`SchemaVersionsUnitTests` proves semantic ordering across digit counts.
Synthetic catalog scenarios substitute the catalog in the real engine graph.

Pin scenarios launch the real host. Selector scenarios share the schema slice's
production registration with `TestConsole`, driving the actual prompt with
arrow keys and Enter. Process tests prove the redirected-input boundary.
`ManifestPinEditorUnitTests` and `SchemaPinCommandIntegrationTests` check exact
bytes across UTF-8, UTF-16 and UTF-32 BOM encodings, both byte orders, CRLF,
Unicode before the token, escaped property names and nested matching names.
The B-045 encoding outline proves that other manifest bytes are preserved.

`SchemaPinUnitTests` proves failure precedence and cancellation cleanup; process
tests prove downgrade preservation even when the subsequent check rejects newer
settings. The encoding regressions failed for UTF-16/UTF-32 before the fix.
No performance benefit is claimed: the comparison required by ADR-0014 is
separate work, and the whole-check benchmark entry point remains unchanged.

### Explicit source selection proof (0043, 0177)

The acceptance bindings in `VersioningSteps.Selection.cs` launch the built
command over synthetic roots. `ManifestSteps` B-008 now reaches the normal
runner entry with explicit disk selection. A separate model load supplies only
the expected Feature list for its assertion.
No step replaces a loaded model's schema set.

| Claim | Mechanism and unit proof                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| B-009 | Required kinds and custom names: `SchemaSourceUnitTests.DiskSchemasWithoutAnEpicDeclaration_WhenLoaded_ShouldUseNamedFilesAndReportDisk`; `SpecSchemasUnitTests.AManifestNamingAKindsSchemaFile_WhenLoaded_ShouldReadThatKindsSchemaFromThatFile`.                               |
| B-010 | Default embedded selection and no disk read: `SpecSchemasLoaderUnitTests.AManifestRecordingNoUpstreamSchemaSource_WhenLoaded_ShouldBuildItsSchemasFromThePinnedVersionAndNotTheFilesUnderTheRoot`.                                                                               |
| B-011 | Resolved provenance: `SchemaSourceUnitTests.OpposingSelections_WhenLoaded_ShouldChooseTheRunSelectionBeforeTheManifest`; `ARecordedSource_WhenSelectionIsResolved_ShouldRetainVerificationAndRejectContradictions`.                                                              |
| B-036 | Manifest validation before override and recorded-source contradiction: `SchemaSourceUnitTests.ARecordedSource_WhenSelectionIsResolved_ShouldRetainVerificationAndRejectContradictions`.                                                                                          |
| B-101 | Run selection precedence: `SchemaSourceUnitTests.OpposingSelections_WhenLoaded_ShouldChooseTheRunSelectionBeforeTheManifest`; `ConsecutiveOpposingRunSelections_WhenLoaded_ShouldRemainIndependent`.                                                                             |
| B-102 | Versioned key/value validation: `SchemaSourceUnitTests.AManifestSourceSelection_WhenParsed_ShouldRespectItsVersionAndAllowedValues`; `ANonTextSourceSelection_WhenParsed_ShouldRejectTheNamedKey`.                                                                               |
| B-103 | CLI selection retains the parsed pin: `SpecSchemasUnitTests.AnOnDiskSchemaSet_WhenLoaded_ShouldKeepTheEmbeddedVersionItsManifestPinsWithThatVersionsRuleIds`; `SchemaSourceIntegrationTests.AValidSourceSelection_WhenChecked_ShouldUseItsSchemasAndReportItsProvenance`.        |
| B-104 | Required-file lookup failure: `SchemaSourceUnitTests.AFaultyNamedDiskSchema_WhenLoaded_ShouldRejectItWithARelativeFileDiagnostic`, absent-file cases for Feature, task and declared epic.                                                                                        |
| B-105 | JSON/schema compilation failure: the same unit theory's malformed JSON and invalid keyword cases for each required kind; deferred reference failures: `DiskSchemaEvaluationUnitTests.ASelectedDiskSchemaWithAnUnresolvedReference_WhenUsed_ShouldRejectItNamingItsRelativeFile`. |
| B-106 | Relative file identity at the loading boundary: the same unit theory asserts `/` paths and no absolute root.                                                                                                                                                                     |
| B-107 | CLI exception-to-stream mapping: `CheckCommandIntegrationTests` retains the existing manifest-error mapping; `SchemaSourceIntegrationTests.AFaultySelectedDiskSchema_WhenChecked_ShouldExitThreeWithRelativeStderrAndEmptyStdout` proves stdout remains empty.                   |

`SchemaSourceIntegrationTests` proves manifest selection, CLI precedence,
older-pin CLI use, reported provenance and JSON-schema conformance, no consumer
writes, explicit upstream verification and rejection. Its failure theory covers
all required kinds and both invalid-file classes. `MockFileSystem` tests pin
the decisions in memory; process tests pin command composition and streams.
Focused selection/reference tests: 57 passed. Full acceptance: 443 discovered,
254 passed, 189 existing pending, zero failed; the command-only
`NuGetAudit=false` override bypassed existing build-tool dependency advisories.
Existing baseline and upstream-copy tests remain regression gates. Reported
upstream address/version fields remain `0001-F3` OQ-6; these tests prove the
existing `upstream` provenance value only.

### D1 test migration

**Implemented.** The existing schema contents, exact-selection,
ordering, version-resource and process-exit assertions below were preserved.
`SchemaVersionsUnitTests.AVersionTheSetDoesNotHold_WhenLookedUp_ShouldReturnNoVersion`
pins lookup absence; the existing unshipped-pin rejection test now exercises
`SpecManifestParser.PinnedVersion`. Inject the test's catalog when
composing its runner rather than passing it to `Run`; both schema-source paths
must reject before local schema reads. Resource tests construct or resolve the
one reader/catalog and keep their shipped-folder assertions. The upstream tests
exercise the folded store without changing digest expectations. § 9 retains
existing names and coverage until those tests actually move.

<!-- last written by: test-writer, 2026-10-14 (item `0219`) -->

`versioning.feature` is linked into `test/acceptance`; [`Versioning/VersioningSteps.cs`](../../../test/acceptance/Versioning/VersioningSteps.cs) binds the scenarios of B-001, B-002, B-003, B-012, B-014, B-022, B-023, B-024, B-025, B-028, B-032, B-039, B-055, B-068 to B-073 and B-075 to B-081, B-086, B-087, B-092 and B-093 and leaves the rest pending for their items. A step one of those scenarios shares with another item's scenario is bound once; that scenario's other steps are not, so it still reports Skipped. B-004's three steps stay unbound: its proof against what a package published is item `0044`'s (§ 12).

A version set is a `SchemaVersions` built in the test from `SchemaVersion` values: the embedded version `0.1.0`, given the `major.minor.patch` number a step or a test names and edited in memory, never a second schema file. "The tool ships schema versions ..." builds such a set, so B-001, B-014 and B-055 run the runner in process over it, and B-055 reads the document `SpecReportDocument.From` makes from that run's report, not a launched tool's stdout. B-002, B-003, B-022, B-023 and B-039 launch the built tool, the only place stderr and the exit code are seen. "The manifest's schema version is `<value>`" (B-039) writes a value of digits alone as a JSON number - the example `1` is the integer the retired pin was, which the claim names - and any other value as a JSON string; the string `"1"` is a unit row. A version is "named" on stderr or in a message when its text stands alone: `0.1` is not named by a message that only says `0.1.0`.
