**Red for the right reason, items `0042` and `0105`.** At `d9d7faa` every test above failed on the missing behaviour - the absent `schemaVersion` read, the unselected version set, the unfiltered vocabulary - with no compile error and no missing fixture; at `7e99515` the same tests pass, unchanged. At `64dbb4e` item `0105`'s unit tests for B-014 failed on the `NotImplementedException` of the member each calls, `SpechtRunner.Evaluate` or `SpecSchemas.Version`, declared with that body by the test commit as item `0034`'s was (`0001-F1` § 8); at `4f1f704`, where the two members are built, the same tests pass, unchanged. The on-disk load's rejection of a pin the tool does not ship (§ 7) is pinned there too, by `SpecSchemasUnitTests`; B-003's row is unchanged, since the check does not take that load.

**No red on record, item `0120`.** This item's tests and its production code landed together in one commit, `6f64a28`. No commit holds the tests without the code, so history records no red state of this item. The tests in that commit are written against `SemanticVersion`, against `SchemaVersion.Number`, `SpecStructure.SchemaVersion` and `SpechtReport.SchemaVersion` as `SemanticVersion`, against `SchemaVersions.Select(SemanticVersion)`, and against the document's `schemaVersion` as a string. The same commit carries the bindings for the steps decision 0005 reworded to `major.minor.patch` in the eight scenarios above, and for the `engine.feature` Background spec-author reworded in `aaf2f5e`; `reqnroll.json` reports a scenario with an unbound step as Skipped, not failed.

**The upstream schema source, item `0176`.** The mechanism of B-068 to B-076 is one read in `SpecManifest.Load`, so every unit test is a manifest built in memory, given a `spechtSchema` with one fault or none, and loaded through a `MockFileSystem`; the source is synthetic, at `https://schemas.example/`, and nothing is fetched. A rejection is asserted as a `SpechtManifestException` whose message holds the member or the kind in quotes, the way the loader's other faults name a key, and not as § 7's whole sentence. The six bound scenarios write the same record into a synthetic root's manifest and launch the built tool: "the manifest is rejected" is exit `3`, and "the rejection names ..." reads stderr for `'location'`, `'version'`, `'hashes'`, `'task'` or `'epic'`. "A folder on the machine" is written as a relative folder, since no fixture carries an absolute path. Three scenarios are not this item's to turn green, and each row is `Partial` on its unit test until then: B-068's last step reads the report document and B-075's needs the check to read the local copy, both item `0092`'s, and B-074's two run `upgrade`, item `0093`'s. B-075's first step is the one B-076 shares, so it is bound, and its other steps are not.

**Red, item `0176`.** The tests name `SpecStructure.SpechtSchema`, `UpstreamSchemaSource.Location`, `Version` and `Files`, and `UpstreamSchemaFile.Kind`, `Address` and `Hash`. Until `implementer` declares them `test/tests` does not compile, on `CS1061` for `SpechtSchema` alone, so the tests by themselves are red on a compile error and not on behaviour: this role adds no production member. With the property standing in as null, outside `src/` and not committed, the two classes ran 151 cases and 22 failed, each on its assertion: the record, the three addresses and the two rows of fetched kinds on a null `SpechtSchema`, and the sixteen rejection cases on a message that reads "the engine does not know the key 'spechtSchema'" and names no member and no kind. The thirteen cases of the six bound scenarios fail on the same message in stderr. Two things are green before the code exists and prove nothing until it does: `AManifestWithNoSpechtSchema_WhenLoaded_ShouldRecordNoUpstreamSchemaSource`, and the five new rows of the input-failure theory, because a manifest carrying a key the engine does not know already exits `3` with an empty stdout.

Hard to test, item `0176`, reported to `implementer`: the command tester sees stdout and the exit code only, so an unknown `spechtSchema` and a rejected one are the same result through the command, and the integration rows and "the manifest is rejected" cannot tell them apart; only the message can, at the unit tier and in stderr. Whether the manifest declares the epic grammar is read from the manifest's text inside the load, so it is reached only through `SpecManifest.Load`, not on its own.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0176`: the order of `Files`, and that no fault repeats the value written, which § 7 states and no claim does; a `spechtSchema` or a `hashes` that is not a JSON object, and a JSON `null` in the key's place (§ 7); a member other than the three and a hash for a kind that is not fetched (OQ-46, open); several faults reported in one rejection, and an absent member raising only its own fault; a `location` followed by a file name the default manifest fills in, where the manifest names none.

**The check under a recorded upstream schema source, item `0092`.** Three mechanisms sit beneath B-024, B-028, B-032 and B-077 to B-081, and each has its own unit class. The read of one file is `SpecManifestStore` over bytes in a `MockFileSystem`. The comparison is `UpstreamSchemaCopy.Verify` over bytes the test builds and a source built with `UpstreamSchemaSourceFixture` and `UpstreamSchemaFileFixture`, beside the tests in `test/tests/Manifest/`; a recorded hash is always the SHA-256 of bytes the test holds, never a digest written out. The selection is `SpecSchemasLoader` over a manifest and schema files in a `MockFileSystem`: the pinned version is a `SchemaVersionFixture`, whose schemas accept anything, and the local copy is a schema that accepts one constant, so one evaluation says which text a schema was built from. A rejection is asserted as a `SpechtManifestException` whose message holds the file's path under the root with `/` separators and, for an absent file, the text `specht init` - not the whole sentence, so OQ-57 can go either way. B-025 has no mechanism to unit-test (the table names the members read); its proof is the two the owner ruled on 2026-10-12 (OQ-39): `EngineAssembly.NetworkReferencesIn` over the engine's library and the tool's own assembly, and a launch with the network taken away.

The eleven scenarios launch the built tool. Each Given that records a source writes a `spechtSchema` holding the SHA-256 of each frontmatter schema file the root holds. "The root holds the copy of that source under its schema folder" makes the Feature schema of that copy require a key the embedded one does not, records that copy's hashes and writes a Feature without the key, so "the frontmatter is validated against the local copy" is a frontmatter violation on that Feature in the document. "The item schema" is the `task` kind: absent is its file deleted, and edited is one byte appended after its hash was recorded. "The manifest declares no epic grammar" takes `identifiers.epic` out of the root's manifest. "Exactly version 0.1.0's rule ids are evaluated" reads the document's `rulesEvaluated` against the size of that version's vocabulary, the only thing the document says about it; that the ids are that version's is the loader's test and B-014's. "The network is unavailable" points the launched process's six proxy variables at a closed loopback port, through an overload of `AcceptanceTool.Launch` that takes an environment; "the check completes" is exit `0` or `1` with the document on stdout; "no network call was attempted" is the same read of the two built assemblies as the integration test. "The standard error names the item schema's file" looks for `.specht/schema/frontmatter/task.schema.json` as written, and "the command that fetches it" for `specht init`.

**Green after implementation, item `0092`.** The full build passes after integrating `0095`, including the schema store, byte verifier, loader, report mapping and four network-boundary cases. The existing scenarios for B-024, B-025, B-028, B-032 and B-077 to B-081 are bound and pass; B-068 and B-075 now pass over the implemented copy.

**Red record before implementation, item `0092`.** The test pass added no production member. `test/acceptance` compiles: of the 127 cases of `versioning.feature`, 35 ran and 92 are Skipped, and eight of the 35 fail, each on its assertion - B-024 on a document with no frontmatter violation, B-032 and B-068 on a document that says `embedded`, B-077 and B-080 on exit `0`, and B-078, B-079 and B-081 on an empty stderr. Three of this item's scenarios are green before the code exists and prove nothing until it does: B-025, since nothing reaches the network today; B-028, since the embedded version's vocabulary is evaluated either way; and B-075, since the check completes without reading any copy. `test/tests` does not compile: seven files name `SpecManifestStore`, `UpstreamSchemaCopy`, a `SpecSchemasLoader` constructor taking the file system and the store, `SpecSchemas.Source` and `SpechtReport.SchemaSource`, and the only errors are `CS0246`, `CS1729`, `CS1061` and `CS0117` for those five. Before those files were written the tests that need no new member ran: the two new rows of the input-failure theory failed on a stdout that carries the summary, the `--json` test of B-032 failed, and the three cases of `CheckCommandNetworkIntegrationTests` passed, as they do from the start.

Hard to test, item `0092`, reported to `implementer`: a call that is not made leaves nothing to observe, so the launch under dead proxies shows only that the check does not need the network, and the guard checks the two assemblies without reading the libraries beside them, and nothing pins that the schema library is never asked to resolve a reference a fetched schema makes to another address. After integrating item `0095`, the guard permits only `UpstreamSchemaFetcher.Fetch`'s `HttpClient.GetByteArrayAsync` and the host's client constructors and credential-free configuration setters. The known network type references are permitted, and another call to the same fetch API is detected by `AFetchOutsideTheApprovedAdapter_WhenItsReferencesAreRead_ShouldRemainAForbiddenNetworkReference`; the same step is shared with the upgrade scenarios of B-033 and B-067. `UpstreamSchemaCopy.Verify` is handed no root, so that a message carries none is asserted at the loader, over an absolute root the mock file system gives. `SpechtReport.SchemaSource` is an init property, which the generated `SpechtReportFixture` does not set, so a test sets it with a `with` expression. The command tester still sees no stderr, so B-078, B-079 and B-081 reach the process only by launching it.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0092`: the whole sentence of either rejection, and whether the one for a file that does not match names the kind, a hash or a command; whether `--schemas` is named (OQ-57, open); the order of several faults, which B-078 and B-081 are read as naming one by one; a fault of the manifest beside a fault of the copy; a local file that matches its hash and is not a JSON Schema, or carries a byte-order mark; what the document says of the source's address and version (`0001-F3` OQ-6, open). Selected on-disk provenance (B-011) is now proved by item `0043`'s tests above. B-075 says which kinds are fetched and no claim says which schema the check uses for a kind that is not: the loader's test pins the pinned version's, as the approved plan has it, and the scenario leaves the root's epic schema in place - a gap for `spec-author`.

Hard to test, reported to `implementer`: the check writes stderr through `Console.Error`, not the injected console, so B-003's and B-039's stderr is seen only by launching the tool. B-002, B-003 and B-039 launch the tool over the real embedded set, which holds `0.1.0` alone; B-003's Given asserts that, and fails the day a second version ships. The launched tool cannot be given a version set, so B-055's exactness is proved at the engine and in process, never through the tool's stdout. The frontmatter rule also reports a root-level violation with no identifier beside the keyed one, so a test asserts the keyed violation is present rather than the exact list. The engine gives no member saying which `v<n>` folder a `SchemaVersion` was read from, and from this item `Number` is no longer that integer: the slot test here and the rule page tests (`0001-F3` § 8) find the folder by schema text, through `test/tests/Shared/EmbeddedFolder.cs`, until a member or item `0126`'s rename replaces it.

Unclaimed, so untested: whether a prerelease or build suffix is a version (OQ-15); whether a part with a leading zero (`0.01.0`) is a version (OQ-28, open); a part with a sign, surrounding whitespace, or a number too large for an integer; and what the report names for a pin under a pin policy (B-053, B-054, item `0124`).

`VersioningSteps.Lock` executes B-092 and both B-093 scenarios over synthetic files on disk, calling the same method as the build target. The dedicated `test/build.tests` project references the build assembly and is included in the solution's tier targets. B-092's numeric newest selection and preservation of previous entries are pinned by `SchemaVersionLockUnitTests.AnUnlockedNewestVersion_WhenLocked_ShouldAddOnlyItsDigestAndKeepExistingEntries`; initial lock creation and unchanged input bytes by `SchemaVersionLockIntegrationTests.NoLockFile_WhenTheNewestVersionIsLocked_ShouldCreateItsDigestAndLeaveTheVersionFilesAlone`. B-093's early return is pinned by `SchemaVersionLockUnitTests.AnAlreadyLockedVersion_WhenLockedAgain_ShouldKeepTheLocksBytes`, with unchanged and edited files. The shared digest mechanism is pinned by `AVersionAcrossEveryShippingTree_WhenHashed_ShouldHashOrdinalPathsAndExactBytes`, `AChangedVersionFile_WhenHashed_ShouldChangeTheDigest`, and `AChangedVersionFileSet_WhenHashed_ShouldChangeTheDigest`, using `MockFileSystem` with repository-relative paths. The shipped-set check of B-089 is item `0044`'s, below.

<!-- last written by: test-writer, 2026-10-28 (item `0189`) -->

`VersioningSteps.Cut` executes B-094 to B-100 over the lock steps' synthetic tree on disk - a frontmatter schema stating its B-087 `$id`, a manifest stating `schemaVersion`, a template and a rule page with a Schema version row - calling `SchemaVersionCut.CutAsync` as the target does. `WhenVersionIsCut` records the exception; `ThenTheCutFails` requires the `InvalidOperationException` of § 7, and every other Then rethrows a recorded failure first, so no scenario passes over a cut that did not run. One mechanism per claim, each with its unit test over `MockFileSystem`: B-094, the lock check before any write, `SchemaVersionCutUnitTests.AShippedVersionTheLockDoesNotRecord_WhenCut_ShouldFailAndCreateNoFile`; B-095, the parse and the numeric comparison, `ANamedVersionNotHigherOrNotMajorMinorPatch_WhenCut_ShouldFailAndCreateNoFile` over `RefusedCutVersions` (`0.2.0`, `0.1.5`, `2`, empty) and `AVersionHigherOnlyByNumericOrder_WhenCut_ShouldCreateItsFolder` (`0.10.0` over `0.9.0`); B-096, the recursive file plan per tree, `TheNewestVersionInEveryTree_WhenCut_ShouldGiveEachTreeOneFileForEachOfItsFilesAndNoOther` and `ATreeWithoutTheNewestVersion_WhenCut_ShouldGetNoFolderForTheNewVersion`; B-097 and B-098, the two splices, `EachFrontmatterSchemaOfTheNewestVersion_WhenCut_ShouldDeclareTheAddressOfTheNewVersion` and `EachRulePageOfTheNewestVersion_WhenCut_ShouldGiveTheNewVersionAsItsSchemaVersion`; B-099, every other byte kept, `AFileOfTheNewestVersion_WhenCut_ShouldKeepEveryByteButTheStatedVersion` over `CutFileBytes` - a schema and a rule page with a byte order mark and CRLF, the rule page also giving the newest version in prose and in a second table cell that the cut leaves as they are, a template that is not UTF-8, and the manifest copied with its `schemaVersion` (OQ-67's proposed default (a); reading (b) changes that row and the B-099 step); B-100, nothing outside the new folders, `FilesOutsideTheNewVersionsFolders_WhenCut_ShouldBeNeitherCreatedChangedNorDeleted`, with the lock and `.specht/` among those files. `SchemaVersionCutIntegrationTests.AShippingTreeOnDisk_WhenTheNextVersionIsCut_ShouldWriteOnlyTheNewFoldersAndLeaveNoStaging` runs the staging and `Directory.Move` on disk and compares every file and folder after the cut. Unclaimed, so untested: § 7's step 4, a `v<new>` folder that already exists; step 5's refusal of a schema or rule page whose token does not occur exactly once; the order of two refusals. Hard to test, reported to `implementer`: the clean-up after a failure between two moves needs a file system that fails on the second move, and no test injects one.

<!-- last written by: test-writer, 2026-10-28 (item `0290`) -->

**The templates an upstream source hashes, item `0290`.** The mechanism of B-125 to B-127 and B-131 is one read, `SpecManifestParser`'s `Templates`, called from the same `spechtSchema` read as B-068's, so every unit test is a manifest built in memory with a `templates` member and loaded through a `MockFileSystem`, over the synthetic source at `https://schemas.example/`; nothing is fetched. Each sound member becomes an `UpstreamTemplateFile` on `UpstreamSchemaSource.Templates`. A value outside B-073's `IsSha256` form, or a file name the pinned `SchemaVersion.Templates` does not hold, is a fault naming that file name in quotes, asserted as a `SpechtManifestException` whose message holds the name and not the name of a sound `adr.md` beside it - not as § 7's whole sentence. What a version ships is read from the engine's own resources: `src/specht/specht.csproj` embeds each file of `src/tool/templates/v<version>/` as `templates/v<version>/<file>`, and `SchemaVersions` fills `SchemaVersion.Templates` from those names. A test that builds its own set gives `SchemaVersionFixture` its templates with `WithTemplates`; the fixture's default is none. `VersioningSteps.UpstreamTemplates.cs` binds the scenarios of B-125 to B-127 over the recorded source item `0176`'s steps write and launches the built tool: B-125's "the manifest is accepted" is exit `0` or `1`, and "the feature template is one the source supplies" parses the root's manifest with the engine's parser; B-126 and B-127 read exit `3` and the template's file name in quotes on stderr. B-131's scenario runs `upgrade` and stays unbound until item `0291` fetches a template, so its row is `Partial` on its unit test, as B-074's is. The `0001-F1` B-004 golden baseline is unchanged: the embedded templates add no rule and no verdict, and no baseline test changed.

**Red observed, not committed, item `0290`.** On 2026-10-28 the tests ran with only the members they name declared - `UpstreamTemplateFile`, `UpstreamSchemaSource.Templates`, `SchemaVersion.Templates` and `SchemaVersionFixture.WithTemplates` - and the parser not yet reading `templates`: over the new and amended tests in `test/tests`, 12 failed and 45 passed. Every new unit test of B-125, B-126, B-127 and B-131, the embedded-templates test, and the B-126 and B-127 rows of the input-failure theory failed on behaviour, an empty `Templates` or an exit code other than `3`; the B-127 file-name theory was outside that run. No commit holds that state. The implementation changed one existing test: `SchemaVersionRulePagesUnitTests.TheEngine_WhenItsEmbeddedMarkdownIsCounted_ShouldEmbedNoPageAShippedVersionDoesNotGive` now counts only `rules/` resources, since the engine now embeds the templates as Markdown too.

Hard to test, item `0290`, reported to `implementer`: every shipped version, `0.1.0`, `0.2.0` and `0.2.1`, ships the same four templates, so B-127's dependence on the pin is reached only over a synthetic set, and the launched tool cannot be given one. The engine's templates are linked from the tool's shipping copy, and `SchemaVersionsUnitTests` compares them with the tool assembly's own `templates/` resources so the two cannot drift. The check reports nothing about the templates it read, so B-125's scenario sees only an accepted manifest through the tool and reads the templates from a second parse in the test process. The command tester sees stdout and the exit code only, so the B-126 and B-127 integration rows cannot tell this rejection from another; only the message can, at the unit tier and in stderr.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0290`: a `templates` that is not a JSON object, and an empty one; the order of `Templates`; several template faults in one rejection; a template hashed under a pin the set does not hold, which B-003 rejects. Claimed and not yet pinned: a B-126 value that is not a JSON string, which the read faults as it does a string in another form; every row of the theory is a string.

**Manifest keys and the listing, item `0047`.** The mechanism of B-016 is one byte edit, `ManifestKeyEditor.AddMissing(bytes, shipped)`, so every unit test of it is a pair of manifests built in memory as UTF-8 text, a consumer's and a shipped one, and an exact expected result: the keys the shipped one defines and the consumer's lacks, in shipped order, each as the shipped text writes it, after the last member, and every original byte kept. Its rows are `MissingManifestKeys` - several keys in shipped order with `+` and a non-ASCII value kept as written, an empty object, a present key the shipped manifest also defines, a present `null`, a key whose name the consumer escapes, a defined name that occurs only inside another value, and `identifiers` lacking beside two keys that are added around it; `CompleteManifestKeys` - nothing missing, a present `null`, a shipped manifest defining only `schemaVersion` and `$`-prefixed keys, and one whose only key the consumer lacks is `identifiers`, each returning the same array; `ManifestKeyEncodings` - UTF-8 with and without its BOM, UTF-16 and UTF-32 in both byte orders, over CRLF text; and `NonObjectManifests`. One fact runs § 7's example over the engine's embedded `manifest/v0.2.0/manifest.json`, expecting it without `$comment` and without `identifiers` (B-016 as amended, OQ-68). B-017's, B-059's and OQ-64 (a)'s mechanism is the writer's plan, so `UpgradeWriterUnitTests` runs the resolved writer over a `MockFileSystem` and reads `UpgradeResult.Rewritten`; the listing and the exit code are the command's, so `UpgradeCommandIntegrationTests` reads the command tester's output as lines and compares them whole, which also proves no diff is printed. The shipped manifest each test needs is read from `ShippedFiles.At` or the engine's resource, never written out by hand. `VersioningSteps.Upgrade.cs` binds the scenarios of B-016, both of B-017, and B-059 over the acceptance tree, whose manifest is this repository's live one less `rules`, `claim`, `tests` and `owners`, and launches the built tool: "already carries a key ... set to a value of the consumer's" sets `exclusions`, so `claim` is the key B-016's scenario sees added; "lacks a key ... defines" removes the shipped manifest's last defined key; "every other key ... is added" asserts each defined key absent before the run now has the shipped value, and fails on an empty set; and "carries no diff" asserts every non-empty stdout line names the pin or a file whose bytes changed.

**Item `0045`'s fixtures, item `0047`.** A manifest of only `schemaVersion` now lacks every key its pin defines, so each `0045` test that expects "current", an empty `Rewritten`, or a `Rewritten` without the manifest holds the shipped manifest at its pin (OQ-64 (a)): `UpgradeWriterUnitTests`' first, second, third and last tests use the shipped `0.1.0` manifest, which carries no `schemaVersion` and so pins `0.1.0` (B-002); `UpgradeRootSources` lays `schemaSource` or `spechtSchema` over the shipped `0.2.0` manifest, whose `spechtSchema` then hashes the epic schema too because that manifest declares the epic grammar (B-076), and over that manifest without `identifiers`, whose `spechtSchema` hashes the Feature and item schemas only - the consumer without epics, which the full-manifest rows alone left untested; `RootAlreadyAtItsPin_WhenUpgraded_ShouldSayCurrentRewriteNothingAndExitZero` writes every shipped file at the pin, the manifest included; `FilesOfAnotherVersion_WhenUpgraded_ShouldLeaveTheManifestBytesAsTheyWere` writes the shipped manifest at the pin, indented with CRLF, with a consumer `rules` value; and the unreplaceable-template test uses the shipped `0.1.0` manifest. "Its frontmatter schemas, templates and manifest keys are already version ...'s" now adds any key the shipped manifest defines and the tree lacks, so the step says what it does. The `0045` tests that already allow the manifest to change are unchanged.

**Red observed, not committed, item `0047`.** On 2026-10-28, `net11.0`, the tests ran with only `ManifestKeyEditor` declared, its `AddMissing` throwing `NotImplementedException`, and `UpgradeWriter` and `UpgradeCommand` as item `0045` built them: over `ManifestKeyEditorUnitTests`, `UpgradeWriterUnitTests` and `UpgradeCommandIntegrationTests`, 27 of 42 failed and the 15 `0045` tests passed. The 20 editor rows failed on the `NotImplementedException`, the not-an-object rows as the wrong exception; the three new writer tests on a `Rewritten` without the manifest; the four new command tests on output that was empty or said "current", and on a defined key never added. In acceptance, B-016's scenario failed on `claim` not added and both B-017 scenarios on stdout naming no version and no manifest; B-059's scenario passed, since `0045` already exits `0` without adding the key, so its red is the two unit and integration tests above. No commit holds that state, and the declaration is not in the tree: `test/tests` does not compile until the implementer adds `ManifestKeyEditor`.

**Upgrade never opts a repository into epics, item `0047` (B-016 as amended, B-138, OQ-68).** The mechanism is the defined-key filter, so the unit tier pins it on the editor: an `identifiers` row in `MissingManifestKeys` and in `CompleteManifestKeys`, and the pin-only fact over the embedded `0.2.0` manifest now expecting it without `identifiers`. B-138's writer theory runs the reviewer's two failing paths over a `MockFileSystem` holding version `0.1.0`'s Feature and item schemas and templates and no epic schema - a pin with `schemaSource: disk`, and the shipped `0.2.0` manifest without `identifiers` recording an upstream source whose hashes are the two schemas' real SHA-256 - plus the shipped manifest with an `identifiers` lacking `epic` under `disk`. It upgrades, then loads the root through `SpecSchemasLoader.Load`, the stage the check runs, and asserts `DeclaresEpicGrammar` is false; a manifest that gained the epic grammar never reaches that assertion, because the load faults on the epic kind first. The claim states only the grammar, so no test asserts the check's exit code. `VersioningSteps.Upgrade.cs` binds `@B-138`: "its manifest declares no epic grammar" removes `identifiers` from the acceptance tree's manifest, the shape that gains it, and "Then the manifest declares no epic grammar" asserts the launched tool exited `0` and the manifest after it holds no `identifiers.epic`. The `0047` tests that compared every defined key against the shipped manifest - `FilesOfAnotherVersionAndAManifestLackingKeys_WhenUpgraded_ShouldAddThePinsKeysAndNameTheManifestFirst`, `ConsumerSettingsAndADefinedKeyWithTheConsumersValue_WhenUpgraded_ShouldKeepThemAndAddEveryOtherDefinedKey` and the acceptance `DefinedKeys` - now leave out `identifiers` as B-016 does.

**Red observed, not committed, item `0047`, B-138.** On 2026-10-28, `net11.0`, against `ManifestKeyEditor` as first built: over `test/tests`, 7 of 1672 failed, every one on `identifiers` being added - the two new editor rows and the pin-only fact on the added member, the writer theory's `disk` row on `epic.schema.json: the selected disk schema is absent.` and its upstream row on `the 'hashes' of spechtSchema holds no entry for the kind 'epic'`, and `UpgradeRootSources`' two rows without `identifiers` on a `Rewritten` that named the manifest. The writer row whose `identifiers` lacks only `epic` passes before the fix, as the claim's boundary. In acceptance the `@B-138` scenario failed on `identifiers.epic` present after the run. With the filter excluding `identifiers` in a scratch copy, the 49 `Upgrade` and editor tests passed; the scratch copy was reverted.

Hard to test, item `0047`, reported to `implementer`: B-059's scenario cannot fail before the build, because exit `0` is already what `0045` returns for a root it calls current. The acceptance tree's manifest is the live one less four keys, so which `0.2.0` keys B-016's and B-059's scenarios see missing follows from `SpecTree`, not from the scenario; the steps assert a non-empty set rather than name the key. Encoding, CRLF and the exact insertion form are pinned at the unit tier only; the launched tool writes UTF-8 without a BOM in every scenario.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0047`: the layout an added key takes beyond the shipped `\n` and two spaces, which § 7 chooses and the unit rows pin as written rather than as a claim; a `[` in a printed path, which § 7 prints without markup and no shipped path holds; and keys added beside a recorded `spechtSchema`, whose bytes B-034 protects and item `0093`'s tests own.

<!-- last written by: test-writer, 2026-10-28 (item `0046`; review findings) -->

**Input failures, item `0046`.** Two mechanisms sit beneath B-018 to B-020. The first is an ordering: `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` calls `SpecSchemasLoader.LoadManifest` before it reads, plans or replaces any file, so a root that is not a directory, a root with no manifest and a manifest the parser rejects throw before a write. The unit tier pins it on the writer over `MockFileSystem`, each row naming the exact failure the loader throws, so a row that reaches another check fails. The second is a mapping: `UpgradeCommand.ExecuteAsync` folds `SpechtRootNotFoundException` and `SpechtManifestNotFoundException` to exit `2` and `SpechtManifestException` and `SpechtManifestUnreadableException` to exit `3`; it lives inside the command, so the integration tier pins it through Spectre's command tester, with C-7 and brief § 9 held by asserting that standard error carries no absolute path. For B-018 and B-019 the proof is the thrown failure and the exit code: an upgrade that ran against no root, or a root with no manifest, would have nothing to rewrite, so "writes nothing" there is a guard and not what can fail. For B-020 every root holds version `0.1.0`'s frontmatter schemas and templates beside a manifest lacking keys its pin defines, so an accepted upgrade would rewrite the manifest and "writes nothing" can fail. The loader's own decisions - `RootExists`, `ManifestExists` and the parser's faults - are `0001-F5`'s and keep their unit tests in `SpecManifestUnitTests`.

| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| B-018 | `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` loading the manifest before any file is planned, `SpecSchemasLoader`'s root test throwing `SpechtRootNotFoundException` for a typed path that does not exist and for one that names a file; `UpgradeCommand`'s fold of it to exit `2` | `UpgradeWriterUnitTests.AMissingRootOrManifest_WhenUpgraded_ShouldThrowTheLoadersFailureAndWriteNothing` and `UpgradeCommandIntegrationTests.AMissingRootOrManifest_WhenUpgraded_ShouldWriteNothingAndExitTwoWithNoAbsolutePathOnStandardError`, each over the `no-such-root` and `.specht/templates/feature.md` rows of `UpgradeMissingInputs`                                                                                          |
| B-019 | The same ordering, the loader's manifest test throwing `SpechtManifestNotFoundException`; the same fold to exit `2`                                                                                                                                           | The same two tests over the row of `UpgradeMissingInputs` whose root holds no manifest                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| B-020 | The same ordering, the parser's rejection as `SpechtManifestUnreadableException` or `SpechtManifestException`, the contradictory source among the faults; the fold of either to exit `3`                                                                      | `UpgradeWriterUnitTests.AnInvalidManifest_WhenUpgraded_ShouldThrowTheLoadersFailureAndWriteNothing` and `UpgradeCommandIntegrationTests.AnInvalidManifest_WhenUpgraded_ShouldWriteNothingAndExitThreeWithNoAbsolutePathOnStandardError`, each over `UpgradeInvalidManifests`: a manifest that does not parse, a rule set to a level no version defines, and a `0.2.0` pin recording an upstream source with the embedded source selected |

**No red, item `0046`.** The production code these tests prove landed with item `0045`, so every new test passed on its first run. Each was made to fail once by hand, not committed: with the expected exit code raised in the integration tests and in the acceptance step, each row and each scenario failed with the `specht:` line its fault gives - "The root is not a directory.", "there is no manifest at the manifest path.", "does not parse into the manifest's shape.", the rule level, and "schemaSource 'embedded' contradicts the recorded spechtSchema source." - so each case reaches the fault it is named for. The unit rows name the exact failure type, so removing the loader's root test would fail the `no-such-root` row, which would then throw `SpechtManifestNotFoundException`. B-019's scenario was already bound and running before this item; B-018's and B-020's two were pending on their unbound steps. The `.specht/templates/feature.md` row was added after review and passed on its first run.

Hard to test, item `0046`, reported to `implementer`: the exception-to-exit-code fold sits inside `UpgradeCommand.ExecuteAsync`, so it has no unit tier without moving the decision out of the command, as with `CheckCommand`. B-018's scenario launches against a missing path only; the file case is pinned at the unit and integration tiers.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0046`: the text of the `specht:` line and that standard error carries one line, which § 7 states and no claim does; and an unshipped or malformed pin, which is B-021's and item `0048`'s.

<!-- last written by: test-writer, 2026-10-29 (item `0093`; B-027 and B-035 amended, B-139 and B-140, decision 0018) -->

**Upgrade fetches the recorded source, item `0093`.** No test reaches a network. At the unit and integration tiers `UpgradeServices` composes `AddSpechtEngine().AddUpgrade()` over `MockFileSystem` with an `HttpClient` registered first: the test's synthetic `HttpMessageHandler`, or by default one that throws on any request, so no upgrade test can reach the network once the slice registers the fetcher (ADR-0013). At the acceptance tier "upgrade runs" composes the same production registrations in process over the scenario's root on disk, with an `HttpClient` over the synthetic `UpstreamSource` handler registered first. That handler serves content per file name and refuses per file name or for every file - other content, an `HttpRequestException`, or a 401 or 404 status - and records each request's address and headers. Scenarios that need no fetch (B-034, B-139) launch the built tool. A differing local copy is made by recording, for its kind, the SHA-256 of synthetic content the source serves under the file name `frontmatterSchemas` gives that kind. `UpgradeRootSources` now records the SHA-256 of its hand-edited text instead of all-zero hashes, so each copy matches and its rows still prove B-029 once a differing copy is fetched (B-026).

**Red, item `0093`.** Run on `net11.0` before any production change: 14 of the 60 upgrade unit and integration tests fail, and 8 of the 191 schema-versioning scenarios. Each fails because upgrade never fetches and today leaves a recorded source's schemas alone:

- B-026 and B-082, unit: the schema file keeps the local copy's bytes ("contains 14 item(s) less").
- B-027 and B-035, unit: "Expected Specht.Manifest.SpechtUpstreamSchemaException, but no exception was thrown."
- B-027 and B-035, integration: "Expected result.ExitCode to be 3 because task.schema.json mismatch: .specht/ rewritten to schemaVersion 0.2.0".
- B-083, unit: "Expected requests not to be empty."
- In the acceptance runs, B-026 and B-082 fail on the written bytes. B-027 and B-035 fail at "no file under the root has changed", because the manifest is rewritten. B-083 fails on an empty request list.

Before the fetch was built, the B-033, B-067, B-034, B-139 and B-140 tests and scenarios passed, because upgrade made no request. They guard the boundaries the fetch must keep. B-139's was already green: it pins built behaviour under no recorded source. B-074's two scenarios share the steps bound here, but their Thens are unbound, so they stay pending.

With item `0093` built, every one of these tests and scenarios passes on `net11.0`. `UpstreamSchemaFileUnitTests` pins `UpstreamSchemaFile.Matches` directly; it was written after the member and passed on its first run. Its byte-order-mark row fails if the comparison decodes the bytes as text. What these tests required beyond § 7:

- `AddUpgrade` registers `UpstreamSchemaFetcher` (and any `HttpClient`) with `TryAddSingleton`, so the test's client registered first wins. A plain `AddSingleton` would put the real handler behind every test.
- `UpgradeCommand` catches `SpechtUpstreamSchemaException`, which does not derive from `SpechtManifestException`, and exits `3`.
- The fetcher's exception names the source by its address without user information. The steps look for `https://schemas.example/specht/` on standard error and no absolute path.

Hard to test, item `0093`, reported to `implementer`: the production `HttpClientHandler`'s credential settings are invisible to any test that replaces the client. Only the fetcher's stripping and `EngineAssembly`'s network-reference guard cover them; the guard allows those configuration members only inside `UpstreamFetchRegistration.AddUpstreamSchemaFetch`.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0093`: the order in which kinds are fetched; whether a refusal names the kind or the file as well as the source; standard error carrying one line; and user information in an upgrade's error. The fetcher's test pins that last one on the shared mechanism.

**Upgrade fetches the templates the recorded source hashes, item `0291`.** No test reaches a network. The unit and integration tests reuse item `0093`'s composition: `UpgradeServices` over `MockFileSystem`, with the test's synthetic `HttpMessageHandler` registered first, or by default one that fails any request. `UpstreamTemplateRoots` is version 0.2.0's shipped manifest whose record hashes the shipped frontmatter schemas, so a root holding them has no schema to fetch, and one template - the feature template, then the lesson template - by the SHA-256 of synthetic text the source serves, which is not the pinned version's; its `location` carries user information. `UpstreamFetchRefusals` gains four rows whose record also hashes the feature template: every schema fetch verifies, then the template's is refused with other content, an unreachable source, a 401 or a 404, beside a manifest lacking `claim`, so a run that wrote anything would rewrite it. `UpgradeOfflineManifests` gains a row whose record hashes every shipped template by its shipped bytes. At the acceptance tier "upgrade runs" is item `0093`'s in-process run over the synthetic `UpstreamSource`; the record hashes synthetic feature-template bytes the source serves under `feature.md`, and the step that says how the root's feature template stands against that hash writes it. "Its manifest names no template as the consumer's" now reads `consumerTemplates` (decision 0017), not the text `templates`, which a record's `templates` member holds.

**Red, item `0291`.** Run on `net11.0` before any production change. `test/tests` does not compile: `UpstreamTemplateFileUnitTests` calls `UpstreamTemplateFile.Matches`, which § 7's design adds and nothing has built. With that one file set aside, 14 of the 77 upgrade unit and integration tests fail, and 8 of the 191 schema-versioning scenarios. Each fails because upgrade fetches no template and rewrites a hashed one with the pinned version's bytes:

- B-130, unit: the template holds the pinned version's bytes, not the served ones.
- B-132 and B-133, unit: "Expected Specht.Manifest.SpechtUpstreamSchemaException, but no exception was thrown." Integration: "Expected result.ExitCode to be 3 because feature.md mismatch: .specht/ rewritten to schemaVersion 0.2.0", and so for `unreachable`, `401` and `404`.
- B-136, unit: the matching copy is rewritten with the pinned version's bytes.
- B-137, unit: "Expected requests not to be empty."
- In the acceptance runs, B-130 and B-136 fail on the template's bytes, B-131 on an empty request list, B-132 and the three B-133 rows at "no file under the root has changed", because the manifest is rewritten, and B-137 on an empty request list.

B-134's and B-141's tests and scenarios pass before the fetch is built, because upgrade makes no request today; they guard the boundaries the fetch must keep. B-135's scenario shares the bound steps, but its consumer-named template and its Then are item `0131`'s, so it stays pending. Its red step cannot show red: § 7's built `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` already takes every hashed template out of the pinned comparison and fetches none the manifest names in `consumerTemplates`, so once item `0131` binds the scenario, and any unit test it writes for B-135, they pass before `0131` changes anything. That filter is untested until then.

**Review round 1, item `0291`.** The B-136 test now also edits `adr.md`, which no row hashes, and asserts it is rewritten with the pinned version's bytes, so only the hashed file leaves the pinned comparison (C-10, B-062 as amended). Proved by hand once, not committed: with the comparison's filter in `UpgradeWriter.Upgrade` changed to drop every template whenever any is hashed, both of its rows fail on `adr.md`'s bytes and the other 75 upgrade tests pass. "The root still has no feature template", the `@B-141` Then, now asserts exit `0` first.

Hard to test, item `0291`, reported to `implementer`: none beyond item `0093`'s - the production handler's credential settings are invisible once a test replaces the client.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0291`: the order of the template fetches among themselves and after the schemas'; whether a refusal names the template's file name as well as the source; a hashed template the pinned version does not ship, which the parser already rejects (B-127); and how `UpgradeResult.Rewritten` lists a fetched template, which B-017 states for every rewritten file and no claim states apart.

<!-- last written by: test-writer, 2026-10-29 (item `0044`) -->

**A shipped version never changes, item `0044`.** Two mechanisms, both new, both in the build assembly beside the lock. `SchemaVersionLock.VerifyAsync` compares the committed lock with what `ComputeDigestAsync` gives now (B-004, B-089 to B-091, and through them B-013 and B-015). `SchemaVersionNumbering.VerifyAsync` compares each shipped version with the next higher one (B-040, B-066, B-088). Each returns its findings and throws nothing, so one call reports every fault and a test asserts exactly which. Decision 0009's "a test fails if a locked version's bytes change" is `SchemaVersionLockIntegrationTests.TheRepositorysShippedVersions_WhenVerifiedAgainstTheCommittedLock_ShouldReportNoFinding`, beside `SchemaVersionNumberingIntegrationTests.TheRepositorysShippedVersions_WhenTheirNumbersAreVerified_ShouldReportNoFinding`: both run over this repository's own `src/tool/schema/versions.lock.json` and shipping trees on every build, and no target calls either member. The build references no application project, so a version's rule ids are the names of its pages under `docs/rules/v<version>/`. `SchemaVersionRulePagesUnitTests.AShippedVersion_WhenItsRulePagesAreGathered_ShouldNameExactlyItsVocabularyOnePageEach` (`0001-F3`) holds the engine's vocabulary to those pages, so a digest over the pages holds the rule ids B-015 names. The unit tests run over `MockFileSystem` under `repo/`; a lock entry a test needs to match is computed with `ComputeDigestAsync` before the lock is written.

`VersioningSteps.Lock` binds B-089 to B-091 over the lock steps' synthetic tree. "The version lock records it" now records the version's computed digest instead of a placeholder, so B-089's match scenario can pass; B-093 and B-097 share the step and read the lock's bytes, not the entry. "`<file>` of version 0.1.0 has been edited since" appends a line to the file of that kind, and "none of its schemas, templates or rule pages has changed since" does nothing. `ShippedVersionSteps` binds B-004, B-013, B-015, B-040 and B-066 over this repository's own lock and trees, from `PackedTool.Repository`. What a published package shipped is read from the lock, because every published version is locked (A-4). B-004's scenario takes the lock's entries `1.0.0` or higher and requires each in the engine's embedded set. B-013's and B-015's run `VerifyAsync` and require no digest mismatch for `0.1.0`; B-015's also compares the engine's `0.1.0` vocabulary with that version's page names. B-040's and B-066's Givens state the pair's premise, which the fault kind encodes, and do nothing; the When runs `SchemaVersionNumbering.VerifyAsync`, and each Then requires no finding of its kind. B-088's outline, reworded by `spec-author` over two neighbouring shipped versions, binds the same way: "two neighbouring schema versions the tool ships" shares B-040's Given, the premise step does nothing, and "they differ in their patch alone" requires no `TemplateOrRulePageChangeNotAPatch` finding. Both rows hold over `0.2.0` to `0.2.1`, whose schemas and manifest differ only in the version each states and whose templates and rule page texts differ.

**Red, item `0044`.** Run on `net11.0`. Neither `test/build.tests` nor `test/acceptance` compiles: every error is `CS0246` or `CS1061` on a member § 7 does not yet hold - `SchemaVersionLock.VerifyAsync`, `SchemaVersionLockFinding`, `SchemaVersionLockFault`, `SchemaVersionNumbering`, `SchemaVersionNumberingFinding` and `SchemaVersionNumberingFault`. With those members declared as stubs that report no finding, not committed, 13 of the 35 tests in the four lock and numbering classes failed: the four edited-file rows (B-089), the two-unlocked fact (B-090), the `0.9.0` row (B-091), the `1.0.0` and `1.4.2` rows (B-004), the `1.1.0` and `1.0.1` rows (B-040), the `0.1.1` row (B-066), and the `0.2.0` template and `1.0.0` rule page rows (B-088), each on "Expected findings to be equal to" or an empty collection. The rows that expect no finding, and both real-tree tests, pass over a stub, as a guard against over-reporting does. In `Schema versioning` 6 scenarios failed - B-089's four edited-file rows, B-090 and B-091 - and 150 passed, B-004, B-013, B-015, B-040, B-066 and B-089's match scenario among them, each a Then that requires a finding to be absent; B-088's two rows were among the 35 skipped.

**B-088's binding, red by hand, not committed.** With `SchemaVersionNumbering.VerifyAsync` built, both `@B-088` rows pass. With its patch condition inverted in `.build/SchemaVersionNumbering.cs`, so that a patch step between such neighbours is reported, both rows failed on `Expected _numbering`, and the other 156 versioning scenarios that ran passed; the file was restored. With the first half of its comparison, `text[false].All(Equal) &&`, deleted, so that a change outside the templates and rule pages no longer excuses a minor step, both rows of `NeighboursDifferingInATemplateAndBeyondTheVersionInASchemaOrTheManifest_WhenTheNumbersAreVerified_ShouldReportNothingOnAMinorStep` failed on `Expected findings to be empty`, 2 of the 12 numbering unit tests; the line was restored.

**Red by hand, item `0044`, not committed.** The real-tree lock test cannot fail on this branch: `0.1.0`, `0.2.0` and `0.2.1` are all locked and match. Before `VerifyAsync` exists, the comparison it makes was run once by a throwaway test - each entry of `src/tool/schema/versions.lock.json` against `ComputeDigestAsync` over this repository. It passed, and with one line appended to `docs/rules/v0.1.0/SPEC001.md` it failed naming `0.1.0`. The edit was reverted and the test deleted. With `VerifyAsync` built, `implementer` repeated the edit: `TheRepositorysShippedVersions_WhenVerifiedAgainstTheCommittedLock_ShouldReportNoFinding` failed with exactly `SchemaVersionLockFinding { Version = "0.1.0", Fault = DigestMismatch }`, and the line was reverted.

Hard to test, item `0044`, reported to `implementer`: no shipped version is `1.0.0` or higher, so B-004 and B-040 cannot fail over the real set, and their synthetic rows are their only red. Nothing in the repository records which locked versions a package carried, so the lock tests hold every locked version, a superset of the published ones B-013 and B-015 name (A-4); a relock under C-17 is a reviewed edit of the lock entry, which the test then accepts. `0.2.0` was carried by tag `v0.1.131` at `5d087a05…` and relocked after, and the test compares with `main`'s entry and cannot see the package's (OQ-60, open). The scenarios over the real tree read the shipping folders the engine's resources are built from, not the resources of a built tool; `SchemaVersionsUnitTests` ties the two. A change that edits a locked version's file and its lock entry together passes, the limit decision 0009 states.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0044`: the order of the findings; a missing lock file, an entry that is not `major.minor.patch`, and a lock that is not a JSON object; neighbours identical once their version statements are set aside; a template added or removed between neighbours; a pair either side of `1.0.0` with a new rule id, which neither B-040 nor B-066 binds; and a pair that is not neighbouring, which the neighbouring check covers, since a rule id new between two versions is new at some step between them.

## 10. Lessons / Spec Deltas

**Independent review correction, 2026-10-14.** ADR-0014 records the owner's
benchmark gate and evaluation of martinothamar/Mediator. The premature CQRS,
model-reader and host-factory migration is removed; schema behavior uses direct
calls through existing services. Process lesson root `0009` records the scope
failure. Product [lesson 0002](lessons/0002-pin-preserves-supported-manifest-encodings.md)
records the encoding defect, and B-045's scenario now covers UTF-8 and both BOM
byte orders of UTF-16 and UTF-32. Existing claim IDs and input semantics remain.

- 2026-10-29, the repository owner chose the recommended default of five questions of `upgrade`'s output for items `0094` and `0131` ([decision 0019](decisions/0019-upgrade-prints-a-line-for-each-file-it-skips.md)). After the lines naming the files it rewrote, `upgrade` prints one `skipped <path>: <reason>` line per skip, in `init`'s form, and a run that only skips is current. Frontmatter schemas get skip lines only with no `spechtSchema` recorded, one for each present schema the pinned version ships, by its shipped name; every named template the pinned version ships gets one without being read. B-030 and B-061 amended; B-142 to B-144 added, with their scenarios and `Missing` § 9 rows; B-063's "whatever the manifest names" is now the owner's. OQ-1 resolved for `upgrade`: it takes no `--schema-source` (§ 5 row 31). OQ-27 resolved, reading (b): a name the pinned version does not ship is accepted with no effect (B-144; § 5 row 32). Name matching is ordinal on the bare file name, a design choice for § 7, which the implementer owns. `0001-F5` § 5 row 3 follows.

- 2026-10-29, the repository owner resolved OQ-55 as proposed: a lock entry is never removed, and a locked version with no folder is passed over below `1.0.0` and fails the lock check from `1.0.0` on, which is B-004's mechanism. C-17 amended: the entry stays when a later tool stops shipping its version. No claim amended: B-004 states the outcome the lock check proves, and C-1 already lets a later tool stop shipping a `0.x.y`, so the pass below `1.0.0` is design (§ 7) and its test (§ 8). B-004's source cites the ruling.

- 2026-10-29, the repository owner moved item `0124`, the pin policy, out of milestone `0.3.0` and out of `0041`'s children, and listed it under `0.4.0`. B-053, B-054 and B-056 stay this Feature's claims and `Missing`; Tasks records what that leaves for `0041`. OQ-11, OQ-16, OQ-29 and OQ-50 stay open. OQ-60 stays open: the owner noted it and did not rule.

- 2026-10-29, B-088's scenario reworded, its claim unchanged, at the test-writer's request: it named `0.1.0` and the next version above it, which in the shipped set is `0.2.0`, a version that adds rule ids, so the premise failed; on a synthetic set the step would pick the very number the Then asserts. It now ranges over two neighbouring shipped versions, as the B-040 and B-066 scenarios do.

- 2026-10-29, the repository owner ruled three questions of `upgrade` ([decision 0018](decisions/0018-upgrade-fetch-failures-write-nothing-and-absent-files-stay-absent.md)), as `0001-F4` decision 0005 ruled them for `init`. OQ-44 resolved for `upgrade`: one fetched schema failing its hash writes nothing, the schemas that matched included, and every fetch completes before the first write; B-027 amended. - 2026-10-11, the owner decided how specht's own shipped files are versioned ([decision 0009](decisions/0009-a-versions-folder-is-its-number-one-number-covers-the-set-and-a-version-lock-freezes-it.md)), in three calls. (1) OQ-22: the folder is the version, `v<major.minor.patch>`, in the schema set, the templates and the rule pages, and the `$id` segment is the same, before the `/frontmatter/` `0001-F5` decision 0011 gives it. (2) One number covers the set: templates and rule pages get none of their own, a rule page states the number as `major.minor.patch`, and a change to a template or a rule page alone is a patch and a new version. (3) The newest version is the working version until a committed version lock records it; a test holds each locked version to its digest and allows one unlocked version, the newest; one build target locks and another cuts the next version. Resolved: OQ-22. Amended: C-6; B-012; B-013 with its scenario. Annotated, still open: OQ-21 and OQ-30. Added: need 9; A-4; B-086 to B-100 with their scenarios and § 9 rows, each `Missing`; C-16 and C-17; § 5 rows 22 to 26. The tag check that refuses an unlocked version is `0055-F6` B-013. Not decided by either decision of the day, and held in OQ-52: where one version's manifest sits in the shipping copy; B-086, B-089, B-096, B-100 and C-16 are written on every tree a version's files sit in. Raised and not answered: OQ-52 to OQ-56; and `0001-F5` OQ-23, which default manifest fills what a manifest pinned to an older version leaves out. `0001-F4` A-2 and B-005, `0001-F3` A-4 and B-033 and brief § 6 no longer wait on OQ-22. Owed to the `implementer`: § 6's row "Which version an embedded folder holds" and § 7's "The folder is not the version", which describe the integer folder as built and name OQ-22 as open. Owed to the `test-writer`: the fifteen new § 9 rows. `spec_status` is `in-review` until § 1-5 are reviewed.

## 11. Open Questions

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- |
| OQ-1 | The manifest field name and the command-line argument name that select the schema source (carried from `REQUIREMENTS.md` § "Still open", at commit `254aabc`). The `implementer` proposes both in § 7; a decision record ratifies them. Since OQ-7 (2026-10-08) it also covers the field names that record an upstream source: its location, version and content hash. Since OQ-25 (2026-10-09) it also covers the name of the key that names the consumer's templates, and the form a template takes in it. | B-009; B-060 | Resolved in part 2026-10-11 by the repository owner (decision 0008, ratification): the key that records an upstream schema source is `spechtSchema`, with the members `location`, `version` and `hashes` (B-068). It was proposed as `upstreamSchemaSource`; the owner's words: "can we call it `spechtSchema` instead of `upstreamSchemaSource`". Still open: the names that select a source. `schemaSource` and `--schema-source` were proposed 2026-10-11 (decision 0008, proposal 7) and shown to the owner only inside OQ-40's option, and are accepted no further than that. Resolved for source selection 2026-10-13 by the owner (decision 0011): `schemaSource`, `--schema-source <embedded\|disk>`, and explicit CLI precedence over a valid manifest. Resolved for the consumer's templates key 2026-10-24 by the repository owner, as proposed (decision 0017, ratification; § 7 "The consumer's templates key"): the key is `consumerTemplates`, a top-level array of template file names relative to `.specht/templates/`, part of `0.1.0`. Resolved for `upgrade` 2026-10-29 by the repository owner, as recommended (decision 0019 call 2): `upgrade` takes no `--schema-source`; only the manifest's `schemaSource` selects the source it reads (§ 5 row 31). |
| OQ-2 | What `upgrade` does with a file under `.spec/schema/` or `.spec/templates/` the consumer has hand-edited since `init`: brief § 6 says `upgrade` rewrites the files; AGENTS.md § Configuration says neither `init` nor `upgrade` overwrites a file that exists. Rewrite, skip and report, or refuse the whole upgrade? | B-005 | Resolved 2026-10-07 (decision 0001): rewrite the frontmatter schemas and templates; in the manifest change only `schemaVersion` and the keys the next version adds. Claimed by B-005, B-016, B-017. Amended 2026-10-09 (decision 0005): `upgrade` rewrites them to match the pinned version and no longer changes `schemaVersion`, which the pin command moves (B-052). |
| OQ-3 | Whether "prints what changed" is the file list and the version pair (A-3) or also a diff of each rewritten file. | B-017 | Resolved 2026-10-08 by the repository owner: the version pair and the file list, no diff - `git diff` already shows content. Rejected: a diff per file. B-017 and A-3 say so. Amended 2026-10-09: decision 0005 removed the move `upgrade` made from one version to another, so the pair the owner chose has lost its first member; the file list and no diff stand, B-017 and A-3 state the pinned version, and whether `upgrade` also names the version the files were at is OQ-21, the owner's to answer. |
| OQ-4 | Carried from `0001-F1` OQ-1: the version 1 epic frontmatter schema sets `additionalProperties: false` and lists no `title` or `description`, while AGENTS.md § "Documentation structure" requires both on every tracked markdown file. Which gives way, and is the change version 1 (still open to change before first publish, decision 0002) or version 2? Owner not asked. | — | Resolved 2026-10-08 by the repository owner (decision 0003): version 1 accepts `title` and `description` on an epic as optional non-empty strings; neither is required. Rejected: a version 2 change, an epic exemption from AGENTS.md, requiring both keys. B-022 and B-023 say so. |
| OQ-5 | The exit code of `specht upgrade` under a root pinned to a version the tool does not ship. brief § 5 names `3` for an invalid manifest and B-003 treats such a pin as invalid for the check, but README does not decide it for `upgrade`. | B-021 | Resolved 2026-10-08 by the repository owner: `3`, as the check gives the same pin, so one manifest gets one answer from every command (`0001-F2` decision 0003). Rejected: `4`, not found, which is for what the command line names. B-021 now names `3`. |
| OQ-6 | When the manifest already carries a key version `n+1` adds, does `upgrade` keep the consumer's value or write `n+1`'s default? Decision 0001 does not say. | B-016 | Resolved 2026-10-08 by the repository owner: keep the consumer's value; `upgrade` adds only the keys the manifest lacks. Rejected: writing `n+1`'s default, which discards a deliberate setting. B-016 now says so. Amended 2026-10-09 (decision 0005): `n+1` reads as the pinned version; the answer is unchanged. |
| OQ-7 | An upstream schema source: can a consumer pin a schema set that is neither the tool's embedded set nor a hand-kept copy under `.spec/schema/` - for example a schema set distributed as its own NuGet package and restored like the tool, or a local path outside `.spec/schema/` - without breaking the offline and deterministic invariant (brief § 9: nothing is fetched at check time)? An answer touches C-6 (every `$id` sits under `rlittlesii/specht`), the rule vocabulary (fixed per embedded version, brief § 4 and § 9) and B-009/OQ-1 (the source-selection field). Options: no; a restored package; a local path; both. Owner's call, to be decided before version 1 freezes at first publish (decision 0002). | None yet; a claim if answered yes | Resolved 2026-10-08 by the repository owner (decision 0004): the manifest may record an upstream source - a URL or a package - with its version and a content hash; only `init` and `upgrade` fetch it into `<root>/.spec/schema/`, and the check reads only that local copy and stays offline. The rule vocabulary stays the pinned embedded version's; C-6 binds specht's own set only. Options considered: the copy kept in the repository by hand (kept, as the on-disk source); a URL or website fetched at `init` and `upgrade` (taken); a NuGet schema package (taken); a path outside the root (rejected: breaks root-relative paths and a CI checkout); a fork of the tool (rejected). B-024 to B-028, C-8 and C-9 added; C-6 amended; OQ-1 extended. | |

| OQ-10 | Is a published `x.y.z` frozen forever, `0.x` included (C-1, decision 0002)? The owner deferred it behind OQ-9 (2026-10-09). The freeze starts at schema version `1.0.0`, keyed on the schema version and not on the package version (C-12 stands). Before it a published `0.x.y` is never changed in place, a later tool may stop shipping it, and a `0.minor` step may fail a tree that passed; from `1.0.0` on C-1 and C-11 hold as written. C-1, C-11, B-004, B-013, B-015 and B-040 amended; B-066 added. OQ-9 stays open: "stop shipping" speaks of the tool's embedded set only, not of where a schema's `$id` is served. |

| OQ-26 | The shape of the upstream template source the owner decided as a later stage (OQ-25): is one source recorded for each template or one for the set; is it the upstream schema source of decision 0004 or a record of its own; and how do C-8 (who may fetch) and C-9 (a version and a content hash, written only on a match) extend to it? The template source extends `spechtSchema`, with the same `location` and `version` (and, next version, credential) and one SHA-256 hash for each fetched template beside the schema hashes; it is fetched only by `init` and `upgrade` (C-8), written only when its content matches its hash (C-9), and its version is never moved by the tool (C-10). A template the manifest names as the consumer's is never written, whatever source is recorded, and the source replaces only templates the pinned version ships and never creates, modifies or deletes a file the tool does not ship (B-063). Considered and not taken: a separate template record; one record per template; the upstream winning over a consumer-named template. The member's name and key form are spec-author's (OQ-65). | OQ-28 | Is a `major.minor.patch` value with a leading zero in a part (`0.01.0`) a schema version? Decision 0005 says `major.minor.patch` and nothing of how a part is spelt; the question sits beside OQ-15. Readings: (a) rejected - one version has one spelling, so the value is not `major.minor.patch` and the manifest is invalid, exit `3` (B-039); (b) accepted - read as `0.1.0`. Today the engine does (a): `SemanticVersion.TryParse` takes a part only when it equals the text its number writes, and the `schemaVersion` pattern of `docs/schema/report.schema.json` admits no leading zero. § 7 records that as a design choice and § 8 as unclaimed and untested, so a consumer meets an exit `3` no claim cites. Owner not asked. | B-039; a theory row pinning the answer | Open. Raised 2026-10-10 by spec-author in item `0120`, from `spec-reviewer`'s finding in § 12 (§ 10). |

Decision 0005 allowed it when a minor only accepted more; under decision 0007 a `0.minor` step may fail a tree that passed, so a manifest pinning `0.x.y` with `latest-minor` can have its verdict changed by a tool release - the reason § 5 row 8 gives for ruling out a wider float. Readings: (a) it floats as B-054 says, and the consumer who sets it accepts that; (b) below `1.0.0` it floats within the pinned minor only; (c) below `1.0.0` it is rejected. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-054; § 5 row 8 | Open |

A-2 lists the manifest keys `0001-F5` and `0001-F6` add, decision 0003's keys, `0001-F5` decision 0004's loosening and decision 0006's key as `0.1.0`'s, on the ground that nothing was published before brief § 8 step 5. Decision 0007 removes that wait, so `0.1.0` is the set as it stands at the first tag, and a part that lands after it is a later version whose number C-11 gives. Not decided: which parts are held back for `0.1.0`, if any, and the version each of the others takes. The same holds for the embedded folder name and the `$id` segment (OQ-22), which change a published version's bytes if they change after the first tag. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. Added 2026-10-11 (`0055-F6` decision 0004): the first tag now waits for the upstream schema source, so the key that records one is `0.1.0`'s; what follows from that is OQ-40. Added 2026-10-11 (decision 0009): OQ-22 is answered, so the folder and the segment are `v0.1.0`, renamed before the first tag; and `0.1.0` is the set as it stands when the version lock records it, which comes before its tag (A-4; `0055-F6` B-013). | A-2; B-037; B-038; § 5 rows 7 and 17; C-6 | Open |
